=== vb-inf_mix_0001 [real exam | set inf_mix | other] answer=3
STEM: Auroras are formed when high-energy particles penetrate a planet's atmosphere and collide with atoms of gas close to its magnetic poles. This collision produces light which usually has a greenish color. In contrast to the auroras which appear above Earth, Jupiter's auroras are very strong and never disappear. This is because Jupiter has a strong magnetic field that constantly attracts particles. According to the paragraph, which of the following statements about Earth is not correct?
  (1) Its auroras are weak compared to Jupiter's
  (2) High-energy particles can penetrate its atmosphere
  (3) It has a stronger magnetic field than Jupiter
  (4) Its auroras do not appear constantly

=== vb-inf_mix_0002 [real exam | set inf_mix | other] answer=2
STEM: Discussing the vision for the modern city presented by architect Le Corbusier in 1922, urban planner Jane Jacobs wrote: "His city is like a wonderful mechanical toy, and its design – as a work of architecture – is simple and harmonious, very organized and very clear." However, she claims that with regard to the actual functioning of a city, Le Corbusier's urban plan "tells nothing but lies". It does not provide a solution for issues related to the management of life in a city, and is nothing but an imaginary vision drawn up by someone who does not understand how actual cities work. Which of the following best presents Jacobs' opinion of Le Corbusier?
  (1) The impressive features of the city he planned outweigh its shortcomings
  (2) His plan was architecturally impressive, but a city built according to it would not be able to function properly
  (3) At first glance, his plan appeared organized and clear, but in practice it was not so at all
  (4) Since he did not understand how actual cities work, his design was not sufficiently organized and clear

=== vb-inf_mix_0003 [real exam | set inf_mix | implication] answer=2
STEM: Erez boasted to Gadi that he could determine the number of leaves on any bush just by glancing at it. To prove his point, he pointed to a nearby bush and declared that it had exactly 373 leaves. Unconvinced, Gadi wanted to put Erez's statement to the test. However, an exact verification of the statement would have involved counting all the leaves on the bush, a tiring and lengthy process. So Gadi devised a far simpler test to verify the statement. Which of the following is most likely to be the test that Gadi devised?
  (1) Erez will state how many leaves he thinks there are on one half of the bush, and how many leaves he thinks there are on the other half. Gadi will check if these two numbers add up to 373
  (2) Gadi will pick a few leaves off the bush, without telling Erez how many he has picked. Erez will state how many leaves are on the bush after the leaves have been picked. Then the two of them will compare the number of leaves that were picked with the difference between the two numbers stated by Erez
  (3) A passerby will also state how many leaves he thinks there are on the bush. Gadi will compare the number stated by Erez with the number stated by the passerby
  (4) Erez will state how many leaves he thinks there are on a similar-looking bush. Gadi will count the number of leaves on that bush and compare it with the number stated by Erez

STEM: The following two paragraphs are excerpts from a book which analyzes disagreements and discussions: a. "In most cases, disagreements focus on one main idea. However, our world view is structured like a network – each idea is interconnected with a vast number of other ideas – and this network does not allow for any single idea to change drastically." b. "Each side in a discussion expects the opposing side to be open to accepting its arguments. But at the same time, each side is afraid to admit to having made a mistake, which would be considered a sign of weakness. Thus, discussions often turn into sparring matches in which the main objective is not to understand the other person's argument, but rather to be entrenched in one's own position." Which of the following issues is addressed in both paragraphs?
  (1) How to prevent the sides in a discussion from becoming entrenched in their positions and enable them to reach agreement
  (2) Why it is important to be open to the arguments of the other side in a discussion
  (3) How the way in which we make connections between ideas influences our ability to hold productive discussions
  (4) Why disagreements and discussions only rarely end with one side being convinced of the other side's position

=== vb-inf_mix_0010 [real exam | set inf_mix | other] answer=4
STEM: At the beginning of the modern era, the idea that the human body is like a machine was widespread in the Western world. This perception of the body also influenced the development of medicine. So, in fact, medicine developed from the study of sick people. Based on the above paragraph, medicine developed from the study of sick people -
  (1) because physicians and researchers learned about the healthy body by observing machines, and only diseases forced them to examine an actual human body
  (2) because doctors assumed that just as machines tend to break down, so too the human body cannot always be healthy
  (3) because comparing the human body to a machine was a mistake and caused many mishaps when trying to heal the body
  (4) because medical knowledge is acquired by examining various diseases and not by observing healthy people

=== vb-inf_v2_0001 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=1
STEM: In an essay on the reading of poetry the author wrote: "Whoever believes that a reader can draw out everything a poem holds by direct encounter alone, with no prior acquaintance with the tradition in which it was written, assumes that in the meeting between reader and poem the reader is always a passive recipient — a kind of blank surface on which the poem imprints itself without any participation of his own in the act of taking it in. One hardly needs the apparatus of philosophy to see the error of this conception." Which of the following claims best expresses the conception the author of the passage himself holds?
  (1) The reading of a poem demands the active participation of the reader, and that participation rests on what he already knows of the tradition
  (2) A reader who comes to a poem without prior acquaintance with its tradition will draw out of it more than a reader whose knowledge stands between him and the poem
  (3) If the reader were merely a passive recipient, the writing of poetry would be a pointless act, since nothing of the poem would reach him
  (4) The complete taking-in of a poem depends on the reader's having taken part in its composition, and is therefore beyond anyone who was not present at its writing
EXPL: The author faults the belief for assuming a passive reader; his own view is the negation — an active reader — and the belief he rejects ties that activity to prior acquaintance, so his view retains it. (2) reverses him; (3) draws a consequence he never draws; (4) turns "participation in taking it in" into participation in writing.

=== vb-inf_v2_0002 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=2
STEM: The first cell of a living organism, formed at fertilization, is called a zygote. Compared with the typical cells of the body it will become, the zygote is enormous — so large that it can be seen without a microscope. In the hours after fertilization it divides, and its daughter cells divide again, and so on through many rounds; but during these early divisions the cells do not feed and do not grow between one division and the next, so that from a single cell of great size there results, within a few days, a cluster of several thousand cells of the size characteristic of most cells in the body. Based on the passage, during the early divisions —
  (1) the zygote divides into daughter cells each of which is equal to it in size, until the cluster reaches its final number
  (2) a rapid succession of divisions takes place, in which each division produces cells smaller than the cell that divided
  (3) the zygote divides into two cells whose size is already that of most cells in the body, and these then multiply without further change in size
  (4) a single large cell splits at one moment into several thousand cells of the characteristic size
EXPL: No growth between divisions and a fixed total mass mean each division halves the size; (1) would require growth, (3) puts the whole reduction into the first division, and (4) denies the succession the passage describes.

=== vb-inf_v2_0003 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=4
STEM: In an essay on the carpenter-builders of the old Baltic towns it was written: "That many of the timber houses still standing along those harbors were designed by a single master, Anders Holm, is known today to a handful of scholars. Such is the fate of buildings that a town adopts as its own: they are lived in, repaired, copied by the neighbors and passed from generation to generation as simply 'the houses of the harbor,' while the name of the man who first drew them is forgotten; no one asks who designed them, as though they had grown from the place like the trees. This was the fate of the finest of the vernacular builders — those whose houses answered the needs and the taste of the town so exactly that nothing in them looked like the invention of an individual mind." What is the central idea of the author's words?
  (1) Holm's houses spread along the harbors because they answered the town's needs and taste more exactly than the houses of the trained architects of his day
  (2) The reason Holm's houses were copied more and more widely, even as his name was forgotten, is that the copyists preserved the skill of the original designer in their imitations
  (3) The fact that Holm's authorship is known to so few is what allowed his houses to be regarded as truly belonging to the town, and so to be preserved
  (4) Precisely because Holm excelled at his craft he received no recognition for it — for the best vernacular building does not look like the work of a designer at all
EXPL: The passage's turn is that perfection in the vernacular manner erases the author: the houses seemed to have grown from the place. (1) explains the spread but not the forgetting; (2) inverts what the copyists preserved into what the passage says was lost; (3) makes the obscurity the cause of the belonging rather than its consequence.

STEM: Everyone agrees that there is a close bond between music and mathematics — not only are the intervals of the scale described by ratios of whole numbers, but more than once a discovery about the structure of music has prompted a development in mathematics. Few, however, notice that this bond is not self-evident and is indeed surprising: music addresses itself to the ear and to feeling, and its material is sound, which exists only in time, whereas mathematics deals with abstract objects that exist in no time and address themselves to no sense. The closeness between the two therefore raises philosophical questions about the relation between what is felt and what is thought. According to the passage, why is the bond between music and mathematics surprising?
  (1) Because it is a bond between an art and a science, and the questions it raises are therefore philosophical rather than musical or mathematical
  (2) Because the objects the two disciplines deal with are of entirely different kinds, and would seem to have nothing to do with each other
  (3) Because it reveals that the abstract objects of mathematics are in fact grasped by the senses, like sounds
  (4) Because music does not merely rest on mathematics but has also influenced its development
EXPL: The passage grounds the surprise in the difference of kind — sound in time and felt, against abstract objects outside time and thought. (1) describes the consequence of the surprise, not its cause; (3) invents a conclusion the passage does not draw; (4) is part of the agreed bond, not of what is surprising about it.

=== vb-inf_v2_0012 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=1
STEM: The historian Laurence Stone wrote: "In the 1930s many historians turned their backs on the tradition of narrative history in favor of what they took to be the ideal way of presenting the past. The reason was, first and foremost, a widely held and only partly justified supposition: that a simple account, concentrated on more or less chronological answers to 'what' and 'how,' contributes little to constructing an answer to 'why.'" According to the passage, what is Stone's position on the writing of history?
  (1) Narrative history is certainly able to shed light on the causes of events, though within limits
  (2) The purpose of historical writing is the chronological description of events, and not the discovery of their causes
  (3) There is no basis for the claim that narrative history concentrates on description rather than on causes
  (4) Historical writing that answers the questions "what" and "how" is unable to provide answers to the question "why"
EXPL: "Only partly justified" is Stone's judgment on the supposition; if it is partly wrong, narrative history does contribute to "why" — but partly right, so within limits. (4) is the supposition itself; (2) and (3) go further than Stone in opposite directions.

=== vb-inf_v2_0013 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=1
STEM: A newspaper reported that the consumers' council recommends buying at the "Everyday" supermarket chain, since a survey among thousands of shoppers found it to be the cheapest chain in the country. The consumer correspondent of a radio program argued that the public should not hurry to follow the recommendation, and gave a reason. Which of the following reasons is NOT suited to be the correspondent's?
  (1) The owner of the newspaper in which the report appeared also owns a chain of supermarkets that competes with "Everyday"
  (2) The survey was conducted by a research company that has business dealings with "Everyday"
  (3) To know which chain is the cheapest one must compare prices over time, not rely on shoppers' impressions
  (4) Shopping at "Everyday" is indeed cheaper than at other chains, but so is the quality of what it sells
EXPL: A newspaper owner who competes with "Everyday" would have reason to suppress the recommendation, not to publish it — the fact gives no ground to doubt it. (2) questions the survey's independence, (3) its method, (4) the conclusion drawn from it.

=== vb-inf_v2_0014 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=4
STEM: In an essay on children's literature it was written: "Good children's literature does make use of the familiar conventions of society, and in doing so it helps children to understand the world; but it also knows not to be bound by them. The classic 'Grandfather Teodor,' for instance, is full of figures who keep the rules of their village to the letter, yet its whole purpose is to set off against them the singularity of the grandfather — eccentric, unruly, and loved by children precisely for that." According to the author of the essay, the figure of Grandfather Teodor —
  (1) helps the young reader to build a picture of the world by demonstrating conventions familiar to him
  (2) is marked by faults that children are meant to learn to avoid, though these faults are what make the book enjoyable
  (3) plays a central part in the book by helping the other figures to break with the conventions they keep
  (4) breaks the conventions of society, and stands out against the many figures in the book who keep them
EXPL: The grandfather is the "not bound by them" half of the claim, and the rule-keeping figures exist to set him off. (1) gives him the other figures' role; (2) moralizes what the passage celebrates; (3) invents an influence on the other figures.

=== vb-inf_v2_0015 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=1
STEM: In many species of plants the seeds lie dormant before they sprout. In dormancy their metabolism falls to a minimum, they lose no water, and they are able to withstand drought. A dormant seed sprouts only when its dormancy "breaks." In most species the breaking of dormancy is not synchronized: seeds formed in the same year may sprout in different years. Which of the following describes a synchronized breaking of dormancy in a species of plant?
  (1) All the seeds formed in a given year were dormant for the same number of years
  (2) Not all the seeds that came out of dormancy in a given year were formed in the same year
  (3) Seeds formed in a given year came out of dormancy in different years
  (4) All the seeds formed in a given year entered dormancy at the same time
EXPL: Synchronized breaking means seeds of one year sprout together, hence equal dormancies. (3) is the unsynchronized case described; (2) concerns the year of formation, not of breaking; (4) concerns entering dormancy, not leaving it.

=== vb-inf_v2_0016 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=1
STEM: In an article on nutrition it was written: "Lycopene, the substance that gives tomatoes their red color, is regarded as the most effective antioxidant in the carotenoid family — a family of molecules that neutralize free radicals. Studies have found that regular consumption of tomatoes is associated with a lower risk of certain diseases, and it is possible that lycopene is the cause. It cannot be ruled out, however, that other substances in the tomato, vitamin C among them, contribute to the beneficial effect." Which of the following claims is presented in the article as an established fact?
  (1) Molecules belonging to the carotenoid family neutralize free radicals
  (2) Regular consumption of tomatoes prevents certain diseases
  (3) Lycopene is the most effective antioxidant in the carotenoid family
  (4) Vitamin C contributes to the beneficial effect of eating tomatoes
EXPL: (2) is what studies "found" as an association, not prevention; (3) is "regarded as"; (4) "cannot be ruled out." Only the definition of the family is asserted outright.

=== vb-inf_v2_0017 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=1
STEM: Scholars of the philosopher Arben are divided over his conception of the purpose of human life. One school holds that in his principal work Arben claims that the purpose of man is the perfection of the intellect — that is, knowledge of the ultimate truth — but that this is a purpose man can never fully attain, since the limits of his intellect do not permit it. According to the other school, Arben held that although the purpose of man cannot be attained, man is obliged to strive toward it as far as he is able. Which of the following claims is correct according to the passage?
  (1) Scholars of both schools agree that, according to Arben, man cannot fully attain the purpose of his life
  (2) The scholars disagree about what, according to Arben, the purpose of human life is
  (3) The two schools differ over the question of what the full attainment of the purpose would consist in
  (4) The scholars disagree over whether, according to Arben, the human intellect can enable man to attain his purpose
EXPL: Both schools say the purpose is unattainable; they differ over what follows from that — nothing about the purpose itself (2), its content (3), or the intellect's capacity (4).

=== vb-inf_v2_0018 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=4
STEM: Researchers at a university hospital found that the hair follicles in the scalps of bald men contain no fewer stem cells than the follicles of men with a full head of hair. The cells in the bald scalps, however, do not function: they fail to develop into the progenitor cells from which a hair grows. Since 2007 the researchers have been seeking a way to make these cells resume their development. Which of the following headlines is best suited to a report of the study?
  (1) "Hair Restored: Experiments Show the Number of Progenitor Cells Can Be Increased"
  (2) "Mystery Solved: Bald Men Lose Their Follicles"
  (3) "Circle Closed: The Follicles of Bald Men Lack Stem Cells"
  (4) "Hope for the Bald? If the Cells Can Be Made to Develop, Hair May Return"
EXPL: (1) reports a success the passage does not claim; (2) and (3) contradict the finding that the follicles and their stem cells are present.

=== vb-inf_v2_0019 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=1
STEM: The minister of tourism recently announced that the state would give financial support to anyone who builds a new hotel in an area that has no hotel but is in demand among tourists. In a newspaper column criticizing the announcement, the columnist argued that the plan rests on a mistake. Which of the following claims is most likely to be the columnist's?
  (1) The very fact that no hotel has been built in an area is the clearest sign that tourists do not want to stay there
  (2) There is no need to build hotels in new areas, since the country already has more hotels than it needs
  (3) There is no justification for encouraging new hotels while the existing ones are losing money
  (4) The minister's task is to promote tourism by many means, and not by financial support alone
EXPL: The plan assumes areas that are "in demand but without a hotel" exist; (1) denies exactly that. (2), (3) and (4) object to the plan without touching its premise.

=== vb-inf_v2_0020 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=2
STEM: In an article on professional ethics it was written: "Psychologists employed in organizations such as the school system are in a position of dual loyalty. As psychologists they are bound first of all to the welfare of the person they treat, but as employees of the organization they are also bound to its interests. This position is liable to impair the psychologist's professional judgment." Which of the following claims is implied by the passage?
  (1) In a position of dual loyalty the professional judgment of the psychologist is necessarily impaired
  (2) The needs of the person treated do not always coincide with the interests of the organization that employs the psychologist
  (3) A psychologist employed by an organization ought to be bound first of all to the organization's interests
  (4) In practice, only psychologists employed in the school system find themselves in a position of dual loyalty
EXPL: A loyalty can be dual only where the two claims can conflict. (1) turns "liable to" into "necessarily"; (3) reverses the stated order of duties; (4) turns an example into the only case.

=== vb-inf_v2_0021 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=1
STEM: In an article on the medicine of a mountain tribe it was written: "When the healer of the tribe treated a sick person, he used both supernatural means — charms and incantations — and physiological treatments, such as herbs, bloodletting and the application of leeches. This combination of magical means with physiological treatment may seem puzzling, but in the eyes of the tribe it was entirely reasonable. In their view, an illness might be the work of demons or the result of natural causes, and since no one could know for certain which of the two was responsible for a given illness, it was best to fight it on both fronts at once." According to the passage, what did the members of the tribe believe?
  (1) The means at the healer's disposal enabled him to treat an illness, but not to know for certain what had caused it
  (2) The combination of magical and physiological means was strange, but it preserved a medical tradition worth keeping
  (3) The healer did not know which of his means would cure an illness of demonic origin and which an illness of natural origin
  (4) No illness could be cured by physiological means alone, and therefore the two kinds of means had to be combined
EXPL: The tribe's reasoning turns on ignorance of the cause, not of the remedy: (3) misplaces the uncertainty; (2) and (4) supply grounds the passage does not give.

=== vb-inf_v2_0022 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=1
STEM: Dr. Avi Elizur wrote: "Psychoanalysis teaches that understanding the adult depends on understanding the child he once was; and the historical sociologist claims that to understand a society today one must understand the social conventions it formed in the past. Therefore, ____." Which of the following best completes the passage?
  (1) understanding a person's past is a condition for understanding his present, and the same holds for a society
  (2) the makers of history are, like every person, shaped by their childhood
  (3) societies, like people, must learn the conventions that govern their conduct
  (4) understanding the adult requires examining the social conventions absorbed in his childhood
EXPL: The two claims share one form — past as a condition of present — and the conclusion states it for both. (4) is the earlier error of chaining the two claims into one; (2) and (3) mix their subjects.

=== vb-inf_v2_0023 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=1
STEM: The sculptor Michelangelo created his "David" in the years 1501–1504. The statue presents a figure that was much favored by the sculptors of the Renaissance — David the king — and has been regarded since its own day as a masterpiece; it is the emblem of Renaissance art in general and of Florence in particular, and millions of visitors come to the city each year to see it. Which of the following claims emerges from the passage?
  (1) During the Renaissance many sculptures depicting David were created
  (2) The Renaissance is regarded as the period in which art reached its peak
  (3) The importance attributed to the "David" today is greater than the importance attributed to it in the Renaissance
  (4) The most important work of art of the Renaissance is Michelangelo's "David"
EXPL: "A figure much favored by the sculptors of the Renaissance" implies many depictions; (2) and (4) overreach; (3) is contradicted by "since its own day."

=== vb-inf_v2_0024 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=2
STEM: One of the main purposes of punishing offenders is to deter others from similar offenses. ____. This, for example, is what the case before us teaches, which took place in England in the nineteenth century: a pickpocket was sentenced to death, and as was the custom the town crier summoned the public to come and watch the hanging. And behold — at the very moment when the crowd stood watching the pickpocket being hanged before their eyes, the pickpockets among them were busy at work. Which of the following sentences best completes the passage?
  (1) But no punishment, however severe, can deter would-be offenders unless it is made public
  (2) It seems reasonable to think that the more severe the punishment and the more people who witness it, the greater its deterrent effect; but this logic does not always survive the test of practice
  (3) But this was not always so: in the past punishment was regarded as the necessary revenge of society upon the offender, and deterrence was only a by-product of the wish to punish in public
  (4) It turns out, however, that precisely when the punishment is too severe, it may fail to deter and instead arouse sympathy for the offender
EXPL: The example shows a severe, public punishment failing to deter — which is what (2) predicts; (1) makes publicity the missing ingredient although the hanging was public; (3) is a digression; (4) predicts sympathy the example does not show.

=== vb-inf_v2_0025 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=3
STEM: Over the past thirty years a steady rise in temperatures has been recorded in all regions of the country. In the coastal region, however, the minimum temperatures have risen at a faster rate than the maximum temperatures, whereas in the mountain regions the maximum temperatures have risen faster than the minimum temperatures. Which of the following claims follows from the passage?
  (1) In the coastal region the minimum temperatures are rising, whereas in the mountain regions the maximum temperatures are rising
  (2) The minimum temperatures in the coastal region are rising faster than the minimum temperatures in the mountain regions
  (3) In the mountain regions the minimum temperatures are rising more slowly than the maximum temperatures
  (4) The minimum temperatures in the coastal region are rising faster than the maximum temperatures in the mountain regions
EXPL: The passage compares minimum with maximum within each region; it licenses no comparison between regions — (2) and (4) — and (1) denies the rise that all temperatures share.

=== vb-inf_v2_0026 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=1
STEM: Professor Hopkins wrote: "According to the biblical account, the first steps of the people of Israel on the stage of history were shaped by years of drought: drought drove Abraham to migrate to Canaan and, later, to go down to Egypt. In the course of the wandering in the desert, drought was the instrument by which the Lord punished the people, and the leaders in every generation used years of drought to warn the people against forsaking the covenant." What is the main subject of the passage?
  (1) The part played by rain and its absence in the history of the people of Israel
  (2) The factors that caused the people of Israel to leave Canaan
  (3) The means by which the leaders of the people of Israel governed them
  (4) The first steps of the people of Israel on the stage of history
EXPL: Every sentence turns on drought; (2), (3) and (4) each name a part of the passage as if it were the whole.

=== vb-inf_v2_0027 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=3
STEM: When Darwin published "On the Origin of Species" in 1859, the absence of fossils of "intermediate forms" — creatures with the features of two groups of animals — was regarded as one of the theory's principal weaknesses. Two years later, however, the fossil of Archaeopteryx, a creature with the features of both a dinosaur and a bird, was discovered, and it was seen as evidence of the descent of birds from dinosaurs. Which of the following claims follows from the passage?
  (1) When it was published, Darwin's theory was regarded as weak because it did not deal with intermediate forms
  (2) Darwin held that birds are descended from dinosaurs
  (3) The discovery of Archaeopteryx contributed to strengthening Darwin's theory
  (4) Until the discovery of Archaeopteryx, Darwin's theory was accepted by few
EXPL: A weakness was the lack of a kind of evidence; the fossil supplied it. (1) confuses not dealing with a subject with lacking evidence for it; (2) and (4) go beyond the passage.

=== vb-inf_v2_0028 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=3
STEM: Udi: "The importance of the study of literature lies in its power to help pupils in many areas of life." Yael: "The study of literature is important in itself, regardless of any practical use, and every external justification offered for it only diminishes its value." With which of the following claims would both Udi and Yael agree?
  (1) The study of literature is justified because it develops the pupils' powers of expression
  (2) It is better not to rest the case for the study of literature on its practical uses
  (3) It is important to devote resources to the teaching of literature
  (4) The study of literature should not be compulsory unless its usefulness can be shown
EXPL: Both value literature; they differ only over why. (1) and (4) are Udi's kind of reason, which Yael rejects; (2) is Yael's position, which Udi rejects.

=== vb-inf_v2_0029 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=3
STEM: The nature authority found that the number of pelicans that passed through the country this autumn on their migration was similar to last year's, but that the "pelican-days" index fell by twenty-five percent. The index is computed as follows: for each pelican, the number of days it stayed in the country is recorded, and the days of all the pelicans are added together. To reduce the damage the pelicans do to fish farms, the authority feeds them at a few sites so as to shorten their stay. Which of the following claims follows from the passage?
  (1) The attempt to keep the pelicans from eating the farmed fish has caused them great harm
  (2) Because of the large quantity of fish the pelicans need, the more of them arrive, the shorter each one stays
  (3) If the change in the index continues in the same direction, the authority will be able to reduce the quantity of fish it supplies
  (4) It is better to compare the number of pelicans to the index than to compare the number of pelicans from year to year
EXPL: Fewer pelican-days with the same number of pelicans means shorter stays — the aim of the feeding — so less feeding will be needed if the trend holds. (1), (2) and (4) are not supported.

=== vb-inf_v2_0030 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=3
STEM: In his book on the Italian Renaissance the historian Jacob Burckhardt wrote: "Our subject is the state, religion and culture in the Renaissance. We are aware that we are not dealing with three separate things, as though any of them could be understood apart from the others; but the examination of each of these factors on its own is indispensable to understanding the connections between them, and it is thus that every historical inquiry must proceed." Which of the following sentences best expresses Burckhardt's position?
  (1) Every historian must deal with the relations between the state, religion and culture
  (2) The state, religion and culture are so closely bound together that none of them can be studied without the others
  (3) The separation of the state, religion and culture is artificial, but it is necessary for historical inquiry
  (4) The nature of historical inquiry causes historians to regard the state, religion and culture as separate
EXPL: "We are aware that we are not dealing with three separate things" concedes the artificiality; "indispensable" asserts the necessity. (2) states only the concession; (4) reverses cause and method; (1) is not what he says.

=== vb-inf_v2_0031 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=1
STEM: Dr. Zeidel is known as "the driver's advocate." In his view, whoever says that the human factor is responsible for most road accidents is right — but only in part: "The city planner, the engineer who designs the car, the driving instructor, the official who approves the road — all of these are human beings too, and almost every accident has its origin in a failure of one or another of them. The driver is merely the one who has to cope with all their failures at once." Why is Dr. Zeidel called "the driver's advocate"?
  (1) Because he argues that although most accidents stem from the human factor, most of the blame does not fall on the driver
  (2) Because he argues that the accident statistics attribute to drivers failures that are in fact the fault of engineers
  (3) Because he argues that most drivers are careful, and that accidents are caused by a few reckless ones
  (4) Because he argues that the driver alone bears responsibility for accidents, since he alone is present when they occur
EXPL: He widens "the human factor" to include everyone but the driver. (2) narrows the blame to engineers; (3) and (4) are not his argument.

=== vb-inf_v2_0032 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=3
STEM: Ron read a newspaper headline: "New Study Finds That Candidates Who Wear a Tie to a Job Interview Have a Lower Chance of Being Hired." He concluded that he had better not wear a tie to the interview he had been invited to. When he read the article itself, however, he came upon a sentence that made him doubt his conclusion. Which of the following is most likely to be that sentence?
  (1) "Employers, who in the past regarded the tie as a mark of respectability, today see it as a sign of conformity."
  (2) "According to the authors of the study, appearance should be treated as one of the important considerations in choosing a candidate."
  (3) "The study also found, however, that employers still expect candidates for managerial positions to wear a tie, and regard one who arrives without a tie as showing disrespect."
  (4) "Another finding was that candidates who wore a tie spent less time in the interview room than candidates who did not."
EXPL: It makes the headline's finding depend on the kind of position — which Ron's inference ignored. (1) and (4) support the headline; (2) is neutral.

=== vb-inf_v2_0033 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=2
STEM: Social psychology describes the "bystander effect": the larger the group of people present, the less each of them is inclined to come to a person's aid. It also describes the "identified-victim effect": people give more readily when the person in need is a particular, named individual than when he is one of a number. A charity that supports needy families wishes to increase its donations, and considers the two effects. Which of the following courses of action should the charity adopt?
  (1) Telephoning potential donors and telling each of them the number of families the charity supports
  (2) Approaching people walking alone in the street, showing each of them a photograph of one family and asking for a donation for that family
  (3) Holding fundraising evenings at large workplaces and asking each workplace to adopt one family
  (4) Organizing an evening at which the families the charity supports tell their stories to a large audience, each member of which is asked to give to one family
EXPL: It alone satisfies both effects — a person alone, a family identified. (1) fails the second effect; (3) and (4) fail the first.

=== vb-inf_v2_0034 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=3
STEM: On the basis of the study of languages, the historian Friedrich Schlegel proposed in 1808 that the migrations of peoples could be traced by following the paths of resemblance between their languages: if the language of a people in one place resembles the language of a people in another, the two are likely to have a common origin, and the intermediate peoples' languages should mark the route between them. Schlegel succeeded in showing a connection between German and Sanskrit, the ancient language of India, but he failed to trace the route by which the Germanic peoples had reached Europe. According to the passage, which of the following best explains Schlegel's failure?
  (1) He found among the peoples dwelling between India and Germany no one who spoke both German and Sanskrit
  (2) He held that a connection between German and other languages could teach nothing about the migrations of the Germanic peoples
  (3) He did not find sufficient linguistic connection between German and the languages of the peoples dwelling between India and Germany
  (4) He was not certain that the connection between German and Sanskrit testified to a shared history
EXPL: His method needs resemblances along the route; without them the route cannot be traced. (1) demands bilingual speakers, which the method does not; (2) and (4) contradict his procedure and his finding.

=== vb-inf_v2_0035 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=3
STEM: In an essay on our obligations to the future it was written: "Our attitude toward the future resembles the attitude of the British Empire toward Australia. When the British arrived to take possession, they found a land inhabited by native peoples and nevertheless declared it 'no one's land,' free for the taking. We treat time in the same way: we regard the future as 'no one's time,' and exploit it as we please. Just as the descendants of the native Australians still struggle with the consequences of the doctrine of 'no one's land,' future generations will struggle with the consequences of the doctrine of 'no one's time.'" Which of the following claims follows from the passage?
  (1) The prevailing view of the future as no one's time is justified, since we cannot know whether anyone will be living then
  (2) The arrival of the British in Australia prevented the native peoples from coping with the damage done to them
  (3) The British did not act rightly in Australia, and we ought not to repeat their mistake with respect to the future
  (4) The conduct of the British in Australia is a conspicuous example of the way people regard the future as no one's time
EXPL: The analogy is a warning; (1) approves what it condemns, (2) adds a claim about the natives' coping, and (4) mistakes the analogy for an instance.

=== vb-inf_v2_0036 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=4
STEM: Researchers wished to examine brain activity during the hearing of music one loves. Each participant listened to three pieces — one rock, one pop, one blues — of which one was the participant's favorite. The researchers found that the same regions of the brain were active during all three pieces, but that only during the favorite piece did the activity of those regions occur in synchrony. Which of the following findings follows from the passage?
  (1) During the hearing of a rhythmic piece there was coordination among the regions of the brain that were active
  (2) The degree of coordination in brain activity during the favorite piece equaled the degree during another piece of the same style
  (3) Certain regions of the brain were active only during the favorite piece
  (4) Hearing the rock piece activated in the participants' brains the same regions that hearing the pop piece activated
EXPL: "The same regions during all three pieces" gives (4). (1) substitutes "rhythmic" for "favorite"; (2) denies the one difference found; (3) denies the sameness of the regions.

=== vb-inf_v2_0037 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=2
STEM: In a book on society it was written: "Every individual stands at the center of a system of concentric circles, each of which represents a mechanism of social control that exerts its pressure upon him. When the individual reflects on all the people whose favor he is compelled to seek — from the tax official to his mother-in-law — and is seized by the feeling that the whole of society is sitting upon his head, he would do well not to dismiss the feeling as a passing neurotic fit." What is the central claim that emerges from the author's words?
  (1) At the center of the social circles that surround us stand people who represent mechanisms of social control, and one must learn to identify them
  (2) The people around us, with whom we maintain relations, are mechanisms that exert social pressure upon us, and each of us ought to recognize this
  (3) A person is at times overcome by neurotic madness and feels as though everyone were watching him
  (4) A person ought not to let the mechanisms of social control that surround him, and the pressures they exert, affect him
EXPL: The passage's advice is to take the feeling seriously as a true perception of pressure. (1) misplaces the people at the center; (3) treats the feeling as madness, which the author denies; (4) adds advice he does not give.

=== vb-inf_v2_0038 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=1
STEM: In an article it was written: "Many philosophers hold that philosophy has a unique pattern of development, different from that of other fields of knowledge. According to this view, philosophical thought always progresses in one direction: after the epistemological revolution of the seventeenth century, for example, it was no longer possible to be an Aristotelian, and after the appearance of Hegel it was no longer possible to be a Kantian. In truth, however, the history of philosophy shows that positions once regarded as obsolete may return and become relevant again, armed with new arguments." Which of the following claims does the passage seek to undermine?
  (1) Philosophical positions that have been superseded by others are abandoned once and for all
  (2) All fields of knowledge, philosophy among them, progress in a single direction
  (3) The view that philosophy has a unique pattern of development is held by many philosophers
  (4) New philosophical positions contain ideas that originate with their predecessors
EXPL: The passage grants (3) as a report, does not address (2) or (4), and argues against the irreversibility that (1) states.

=== vb-inf_v2_0039 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=2
STEM: During the First World War the Dada movement arose in Europe, seeking to rebel against the national values that governed society at the time. A celebrated Dada work of the period is the collage of the German artist Hannah Höch, made of fragments of photographs and text cut from newspapers — among them the head of the German president set upon the body of a dancer. The collage was a subversive statement, but no less subversive was the choice of newspapers as the very material from which to speak against the artistic conventions of society. Which of the following claims is correct according to the passage?
  (1) The use of texts from newspapers weakened the critical message of the work
  (2) The criticism of society's values in the collage was expressed both through the source of its materials and through its composition
  (3) Despite the sharp criticism of society in the work, the materials chosen show that the criticism was aimed mainly at the newspapers
  (4) The technique chosen for the work undermined the artistic conventions more than the work's content did
EXPL: "No less subversive" makes the two channels equal; (4) turns it into "more," (1) into a weakening, and (3) into a change of target.

=== vb-inf_v2_0040 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=2
STEM: The former president of the Supreme Court, Justice Aharon Barak, argued in a famous ruling that "all human conduct is subject to a legal norm." He explained that there are areas governed by private autonomy — friendships, for instance, or subjective thoughts — but that these too exist only because the legal system recognizes them; were it not for that recognition, they would be exposed to the interference of all, and the system's decision to leave them alone is itself part of the norm. According to the passage, which of the following claims did Justice Barak make?
  (1) In practice, even the areas that appear to be governed by private autonomy are not, since the legal system permits itself to intrude into them
  (2) The very recognition by the legal system that certain areas of the individual's life are not open to its interference is itself a legal position regarding the autonomous status of those areas
  (3) Had human beings not tried to interfere in areas such as subjective thought, there would have been no need for norms securing autonomy
  (4) Legal norms apply to almost every kind of human conduct, but not to subjective thought or to personal relations
EXPL: Barak's point is that non-interference is a norm, not an absence of one. (1) makes him claim intrusion; (3) adds a counterfactual he does not state; (4) contradicts "all."

=== vb-inf_v2_0041 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=2
STEM: Since 2012 a business magazine has published each year a list of the hundred athletes with the highest earnings that year. In 2020 two women appeared on the list, and although that is a small number, it was the first time since 2016 that the list had included more than one woman. Which of the following claims contradicts what is stated in the passage?
  (1) The list of 2015 included no woman
  (2) The list of 2016 included only one woman
  (3) The list of 2017 included only one woman
  (4) The list of 2018 included no woman
EXPL: "The first time since 2016" makes 2016 the last year with more than one woman; (3) and (4) are what the passage implies about 2017–2019, and (1) concerns a year the passage does not constrain.

=== vb-inf_v2_0042 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=1
STEM: The functional approach to language analyzes speech according to the functions it fulfills in verbal communication. The linguist Roman Jakobson defined six such functions: conveying new information; expressing the speaker's feelings; influencing the addressee, by command, request or persuasion; taking pleasure in the words themselves, as in rhyme and word-play; maintaining social contact; and clarifying the communication itself, as in the question "What did you say?" Which of the following claims does NOT accord with Jakobson's approach?
  (1) The use of rhyme or word-play does not fulfill a function in communication, but is intended only to give pleasure
  (2) There are components of verbal communication whose sole purpose is to maintain social contact
  (3) Verbal communication does not only convey new information; at times its purpose is to express feelings
  (4) One of the uses we make of language is the attempt to influence the actions and conduct of the addressee
EXPL: Giving pleasure through words IS one of the six functions; (1) denies it the status Jakobson gives it. (2), (3) and (4) each restate a function.

=== vb-inf_v2_0043 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=3
STEM: In a book on the history of Jerusalem it was written: "Over the years, archaeological excavations at the foot of the Temple Mount have uncovered many finds from the Umayyad period, a period about which almost nothing was known from written sources, and about which our information comes chiefly from finds such as these. Thus the excavators uncovered the remains of a large palace-city, built by the caliph Abd al-Malik and by his successors Sulayman and Hisham, who inherited the rule after him. The Umayyads, like the Hasmoneans, the Herodians and the Romans before them, built in Jerusalem, and in the footsteps of the Hasmoneans fixed its center beside the Temple Mount." Which of the following claims does NOT arise from the quotation?
  (1) The structures found at the foot of the Temple Mount were not all built in the reign of Abd al-Malik
  (2) The Hasmoneans built in Jerusalem before the Umayyads
  (3) Few archaeological remains of the Umayyad period have survived
  (4) The remains of the Umayyad period found beside the Temple Mount are the remains of a palace
EXPL: The passage says the written sources are scanty and the finds are the chief source — which implies the finds are plentiful, not few; (1), (2) and (4) each follow.

=== vb-inf_v2_0044 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=4
STEM: In an article on the history of religions it was written: "In the method of the men of religion of the Middle Ages, none of the clear and visible differences between the great monotheistic religions is a difference of substance. Some of them disputed, for example, the customs of the rival religions; but as they understood it, no believer in a monotheistic religion could dispute the central premises of the rival monotheistic religions without thereby undermining the foundation on which his own convictions rested." Which of the following claims arises from the passage regarding the men of religion of the Middle Ages?
  (1) They argued that the differences between the monotheistic religions are superficial, and therefore found no fault in the other religions
  (2) They concentrated on trying to undermine the foundations of the other monotheistic religions without harming the principles of their own
  (3) In their method, the differences in custom between the monotheistic religions are differences of substance
  (4) Since they regarded the monotheistic religions as resting on a common foundation, they did not deny the central premises of the rival religions
EXPL: (1) contradicts "some disputed the customs"; (2) is what they held to be impossible; (3) contradicts "none… is a difference of substance."

=== vb-inf_v2_0045 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=1
STEM: Ring-shaped breads with a hole in the middle are an ancient product: they appear already in drawings from ancient Egypt, and from there they spread across the world. The advantage of the hole is that it allows the rings to be threaded on a stick or a cord and carried easily. According to the food historian Maria Balinska, the bagel popular in America today developed either from the Italian "taralli," hard boiled bread-rings, or from the Chinese "girde," which resembles the bagel in shape but has a dimple in the middle rather than a hole. Which of the following claims arises from the passage?
  (1) The Italian taralli and the American bagel share a quality that the Chinese girde lacks
  (2) The ring-shaped breads in the ancient Egyptian drawings resemble neither the taralli nor the girde
  (3) Contrary to Balinska's claim, the bagel developed from the breads of ancient Egypt
  (4) Because of their useful shape, bagels developed independently in several places
EXPL: A hole can be threaded; a dimple cannot. (2), (3) and (4) go beyond, or against, what the passage says.

  (2) The origin of the craving for salt lies in the activity of the brain's reward regions
  (3) The level of activity in the brain's reward regions depends on how much salt the body needs

  (2) Since we cannot examine an event from all angles at once, we can never obtain a complete picture of it

=== vb-inf_v2_0054 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=3
STEM: The economist Thomas Schelling argues that one of the ways to strengthen one's bargaining position is the "voluntary sacrifice of freedom of choice." How, for example, can you persuade the seller of a car who asks 22,000 that you will not pay more than 20,000? You can declare, in the presence of a third party, that you will pay him 3,000 if you end up paying more than 20,000 for the car. Why does Schelling call the declaration a "sacrifice of freedom of choice"?
  (1) Because it means that the buyer cannot refuse to buy the car if the seller lowers the price to 20,000
  (2) Because the buyer chose to involve a third party and gave up his right to negotiate with the seller directly
  (3) Because the buyer chose to put himself in a position in which agreeing to a higher price would be unreasonable for him
  (4) Because after the declaration the buyer can no longer choose to buy the car for 20,000
EXPL: The buyer binds himself: paying more now costs him 3,000 as well. (1) and (4) misstate what the declaration forbids; (2) mistakes the witness's role.

Which of the following claims is implied by the passage?
  (1) The establishment of the liberal-democratic regime is what encouraged women to begin their struggle for equal rights

=== vb-inf_v2_0059 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=3
STEM: Dr. Lavon: "It is the nature of folk customs to undergo a process of evolution and specification. From generation to generation each custom becomes more defined, takes on more shades and variants, and acquires a unique character in each community and each family. Over the years, with this crystallization and refinement, the custom becomes a marker of identity across generations, distinguishing families and communities from one another." Which of the following processes is most suited to exemplify Dr. Lavon's claim?
  (1) The leaders of a national movement succeeded in instilling among the public the celebration of a minor festival, although in earlier generations it had not been customary to celebrate it
  (2) Although in the past the text recited at the spring festival existed in several versions, in our day a single, uniform version has become accepted everywhere
  (3) In our day every community insists on customs of its own for lighting the festival lamps, although at first there were no precise instructions in the matter
  (4) The custom of holding a ceremonial meal on the New Year of the Trees, once widespread, is little practiced today
EXPL: Specification into community variants; (2) is the reverse (convergence), (1) is adoption, (4) is decline.

=== vb-inf_v2_0060 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=3
STEM: The human gut houses a large and varied population of bacteria, most of them "friendly" bacteria essential to digestion. Foods rich in such bacteria — "probiotic" products — enjoy great popularity, but scientists argue that there is no point in consuming them: even if some of the bacteria in these products survive the passage through the stomach, their number is negligible beside the bacteria the gut of a healthy person already holds, so that only a person whose gut lacks friendly bacteria could benefit from probiotic products. Which of the following claims is implied by the passage?
  (1) The bacteria in probiotic products survive the stomach better in people whose gut lacks bacteria than in healthy people
  (2) The scientists warn that a person whose gut lacks bacteria may be harmed by probiotic products
  (3) Consuming probiotic products can increase the number of friendly bacteria in the gut
  (4) The friendly bacteria in the gut are less useful than their name suggests
EXPL: If a person who lacks the bacteria can benefit, the products must add some. (1), (2) and (4) are not implied.

=== vb-inf_v2_0061 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=2
STEM: In the introduction to his book Rabbi Menachem Hirschman wrote: "This book deals with Judaism as reflected in the writings of the Church Fathers. Hirschman's words are intended to rebut a certain claim. Which of the following claims is it?
  (1) It is not right to examine the writings of the Church Fathers from a Jewish point of view alone
  (2) The study of the Christian view of Judaism does not accord with the values of Jewish tradition

=== vb-inf_v2_0062 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=1
STEM: In an article it was written: "If the court takes upon itself the authority to rule that an act was unlawful because in its view the act was immoral, the link it makes may be read in the opposite direction as well: a ruling that an act was lawful may be understood as a determination that the act meets a minimal standard of morality — and so lawfulness may dull the public protest that an immoral act demands." Which of the following claims best presents the danger the author warns of?
  (1) Public protest against an immoral act will fade, since the court will rule that the act is lawful and it will therefore be regarded as meeting the demands of morality
  (2) Public protest against an immoral act will fade, since the court will rule that the act is lawful and the protest will therefore be regarded as unnecessary
  (3) Public protest against an unlawful act will fade, since the court will rule that the act is moral and therefore lawful
  (4) Public protest against an unlawful act will fade, since the court will rule that the act is lawful and therefore in keeping with the rules of morality
EXPL: The chain is lawful → taken as moral → protest dulled; (2) drops the moral inference, (3) and (4) start from an unlawful act.

=== vb-inf_v2_0063 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=4
STEM: Researchers once believed that only mammals with large brains — apes, elephants, whales — are capable of expressing grief. Recent findings show that mammals with large brains do indeed have especially complex ways of expressing their grief, thanks to their developed powers of thought; but the chief factor that determines whether the members of a species can express grief in some way is their capacity to form close and lasting relationships. Which of the following claims is implied by the passage?
  (1) Developed powers of thought are the basis of the capacity to form close relationships
  (2) There are species without large brains that are nevertheless able to express grief in especially complex ways
  (3) The assumption that large-brained mammals are the only animals capable of forming close relationships underlay the researchers' earlier view
  (4) There are species without large brains whose members can express grief, though not in complex ways
EXPL: Complex expression stays with large brains; the capacity to express grief at all follows relationships — so small-brained species with close bonds express grief simply. (1) reverses a link, (2) contradicts the first finding, (3) attributes an assumption the passage does not.

=== vb-inf_v2_0064 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=3
STEM: The following appeared in a magazine for collectors: "From the beginning of her career the potter Ruth Adani signed her work with the mark of a heron, and it was this mark that appeared on every piece she sold. Adani adopted several other marks in its place, among them a reed. When her contract with the gallery expired, she returned to signing with the heron." Which of the following claims about Adani follows from the passage?
  (1) She used the reed mark only after her contract with the gallery had expired
  (2) The gallery claimed that the terms of her contract gave it ownership of all the marks she used
  (3) For a period she avoided using the heron mark in order to keep the ownership of the pieces she sold
  (4) She returned to the heron mark because the other marks did not belong to her in law
EXPL: The heron would have given the gallery her pieces; avoiding it kept them hers. (1) reverses the timing, (2) extends the claim to all marks, (4) invents a legal status for the other marks.

=== vb-inf_v2_0065 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=3
STEM: Liora told Nahum that she had read in a newspaper about a study recommending that pupils be allowed to chew gum in class, since chewing raises concentration and makes the material easier to take in. She added, however, that she did not intend to follow the recommendation, because it offends against good manners and contradicts other studies she had read on the subject. From Nahum's reply, Liora realized that he had not understood what she had said. Which of the following is most likely to have been Nahum's reply?
  (1) "In that case I am glad I did not read the article myself, because I am easily swayed by newspapers and would certainly have adopted this unpleasant habit."
  (2) "I would do as you do; it would take more than one study to make me change, even slightly, the way I was brought up to behave."
  (3) "I am actually thinking of following the recommendation, because if all the studies reach the same conclusion, then in my view habits and manners must give way to them."
  (4) "Knowing you, even if you had not read the other studies I doubt you would have followed this recommendation and given up your manners."
EXPL: Liora said the studies contradict one another; only (3) rests on their agreeing. The other replies each take up something she actually said.

=== vb-inf_v2_0066 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=3
STEM: The following appeared in a book review: "In his book Professor Kaplan draws on his considerable expertise in both Jewish and general history when he discusses the intellectual crossroads between Jewish and Christian movements of ideas. In doing so he breaks down the barriers between Jewish history and the history of other nations — barriers that arose because of the artificial separation, in Israeli universities, between the departments of Jewish and of general history." Which of the following claims is implied by the reviewer?
  (1) Books like Kaplan's prove that the division between the departments of Jewish and general history is not what created the barriers between the two fields
  (2) Kaplan's criticism of the division between Jewish history and the history of other nations would have been valid even had he not blamed the Israeli universities for it
  (3) Were it not for the customary division between the departments of Jewish and general history in Israeli universities, there would be more books like Kaplan's
  (4) Had Kaplan studied each of the two fields in a different department, he would not have been able to identify the points at which Jewish and general history meet
EXPL: The reviewer blames the separation for the barriers Kaplan had to break; remove the separation and such books become more common. (1) contradicts him, (2) attributes a criticism to Kaplan rather than to the reviewer, (4) is not implied.

=== vb-inf_v2_0067 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=4
STEM: Until the printing press was invented, books and scrolls were copied by hand. The scribes tried to make their copies as accurate as they could, but at times they made mistakes: parts of the text were left out or written twice, letters were interchanged, and so on. When the new, inaccurate copy itself served as the basis for further copying, all its "descendants" — the texts copied directly from it, those copied from them, and so on — inherited its mistakes, because the scribes copied the mistakes even when it was obvious that they were mistakes. At times new mistakes were added to these copied texts. According to the passage, why did the descendants of the inaccurate copy inherit its mistakes?
  (1) Because they were copied by hand, a method that is liable to produce mistakes
  (2) Because the scribes were not aware that there could be mistakes in the copy they were using as their basis
  (3) Because they were not copied as carefully as the copies that were based on the original book
  (4) Because the scribes tried to copy as accurately as they could
EXPL: "Even when it was obvious that they were mistakes" rules out (2) and (3); the scribes reproduced what they saw because fidelity was their aim. (1) explains new mistakes, not inherited ones.

=== vb-inf_v2_0068 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=1
STEM: Musical wind instruments are usually divided into two families: the woodwinds, such as the recorder and the clarinet, and the brass, such as the trumpet and the trombone. Woodwind instruments produce a richer variety of tones, but the intensity of their tone is low; brass instruments, by contrast, produce a strong and full tone, but the variety of their tones is limited. Adolphe Sax wished to create a new instrument that would combine the best of both worlds, and thus the first saxophone came into being. According to the passage, which of the following pairs of features characterizes the saxophone that Sax created?
  (1) The intensity of its tone is greater than that of the woodwinds, and the variety of its tones is richer than that of the brass
  (2) The intensity of its tone is greater than that of the brass, and the variety of its tones is similar to theirs
  (3) The intensity of its tone is similar to that of the woodwinds, and the variety of its tones is richer than theirs
  (4) The intensity of its tone is similar to that of the woodwinds, and the variety of its tones is richer than that of the brass
EXPL: "The best of both worlds" means each strength taken from the family that has it — intensity beyond the woodwinds, variety beyond the brass. (2) exceeds the brass in their own strength; (3) and (4) leave the intensity at the woodwinds' weak level.

=== vb-inf_v2_0069 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=4
STEM: In the eighteenth century physiognomy — the doctrine that a person's character can be deduced from his appearance — was regarded as an obvious truth. Today physiognomy is denounced as a pseudo-science, although in recent years it has enjoyed a revival in certain circles that seek to promote a "new physiognomy," a doctrine whose hypotheses are more moderate than those of its predecessor, but which is no less problematic. What can be inferred about the author's opinion of physiognomy?
  (1) Physiognomy, which until recently was regarded as a genuine science, is now regarded as problematic because its hypotheses became more moderate upon its revival
  (2) The "new physiognomy" is not a pseudo-science, since its hypotheses are more moderate than those of eighteenth-century physiognomy
  (3) The moderate hypotheses of the "new physiognomy," unlike those of eighteenth-century physiognomy, have re-established it as a science
  (4) Although the attempt to revive physiognomy has succeeded in certain circles, the doctrine is no less problematic and no less a pseudo-science than before
EXPL: "No less problematic" is the author's verdict; (2) and (3) treat moderation as rehabilitation, and (1) confuses the cause of the verdict with its timing.

Which of the following statements about the riddle is NOT made in the passage?

  (2) The solution of the riddle is four
  (3) The riddle appears in a book of riddles published in the seventeenth century
  (4) The solution of the riddle appears in a book published by Claude Bachet

=== vb-inf_v2_0071 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=3
STEM: One of the projects in which the American space agency is engaged is the search for life on other planets. Recently, scientists working on the project have taken an interest in "ice cauldrons" — cracks in ice that form distinctive spiral shapes, which can be recognized easily even from very great distances. Ice cauldrons are common near the Earth's north pole, where they are formed by the eruption of volcanoes buried under a layer of ice and snow, and tests carried out on Earth show that such eruptions create an environment that combines heat, moisture and a wide range of minerals. A combination of three of the following claims could explain the scientists' interest in ice cauldrons. Which is the remaining claim?
  (1) Since the conditions that form ice cauldrons are not unique to Earth, it is possible that cauldrons are formed on other planets too
  (2) The combination of heat, moisture and a wide range of minerals is regarded as an ideal condition for the emergence of life
  (3) Spiral-shaped cracks in ice may be formed not only by a volcanic eruption but by other causes as well
  (4) The agency has almost no means of searching for life on other planets other than photographing their surfaces from great distances
EXPL: (1), (2) and (4) chain into the interest — cauldrons elsewhere, life-friendly conditions, visible from afar; (3) weakens the chain by making the spirals ambiguous.

=== vb-inf_v2_0072 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=4
STEM: Political movements tend to create myths about the past in order to shape the future, especially in times of crisis. Thus, at the beginning of the twentieth century, when Socialist Zionism was growing slowly and with great difficulty, it glorified the Bilu movement of the previous generation in an attempt to attach itself to historical roots and so to strengthen its own spiritual foundations. By contrast, the Jewish labor movement in the United States was at that time developing rapidly and its activity was in full swing; it therefore had no need to be nostalgic about the Am Olam movement, its own predecessor. The passage above was written in answer to a question. Which of the following is most likely to be that question?
  (1) Why did the difficulties experienced by Socialist Zionism lead it to rely on myths, whereas the success of the Jewish labor movement in the United States did not?
  (2) What significance did the myths of Bilu and Am Olam have for the Socialist Zionists on the one hand and for the Jewish labor movement in the United States on the other?
  (3) What might explain the fact that the Socialist Zionists found a source of inspiration in the Bilu movement in particular, and not in Am Olam?
  (4) Why did the myth of Bilu take root among the Socialist Zionists, whereas Am Olam did not fulfill a similar function for the Jewish labor movement in the United States?
EXPL: The passage explains one movement's myth-making and the other's absence of it; (1) presupposes the answer in its wording, (2) asks about significance rather than cause, (3) asks about a choice between the two myths that the passage never compares.

The rules of the game are clear to everyone, and people have learned to treat the promises made in advertisements with caution. And so I ask: when will people finally learn to be equally cautious when they read reports of scientific breakthroughs in the popular media?" Which of the following claims is implied by the text?
  (1) People should treat advertisements with more caution than they treat reports of scientific breakthroughs in the popular media
  (2) People place too much trust in reports of scientific breakthroughs in the popular media
  (3) In the past, advertisements were more truthful than they are today
  (4) People today are more suspicious of reports of scientific breakthroughs in the popular media than they are of advertisements

=== vb-inf_v2_0074 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=2
STEM: The signature of the celebrated painter Rembrandt appears on many paintings that were done by his pupils. This should not be regarded as forgery, however. In his day the regulations of the painters' guild forbade pupils to sign their own works as long as they were employed in their master's studio; only the master was permitted to sign them. Moreover, the master often corrected his pupils' paintings so as to make them worthy of his signature. A painter's signature thus served to certify the quality of a work. Which of the following is inferred from the text?
  (1) Rembrandt signed his pupils' paintings only if he had corrected them
  (2) In Rembrandt's day a painter's signature on a painting was not proof that he had painted it himself
  (3) Rembrandt's pupils asked him to sign their works, although this was against the guild's regulations
  (4) Pupils in Rembrandt's studio signed their own paintings only when Rembrandt declined to sign them
EXPL: A signature certified quality, not authorship. (1) turns "often" into "only if"; (3) and (4) contradict the regulation.

=== vb-inf_v2_0075 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=3
STEM: The freeing of slaves in Rome was a gradual process, and the first to benefit from it were those slaves whose professional skills in certain trades were superior to those of their masters. From this we learn that the slaves who came from Greece and from other parts of the East — merchants and craftsmen — were freed before the Cimbri, the Teutons and the Lusitanians, who worked mainly in agriculture. According to the passage, why did the Cimbrian, Teutonic and Lusitanian slaves have to wait longer to be freed?
  (1) Because they were unskilled at agricultural work
  (2) Because the Romans needed their highly developed agricultural skills
  (3) Because they worked in a sector in which they did not have the advantage of superior skill
  (4) Because they had not come to Rome from the East

=== vb-inf_v2_0076 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=4
STEM: In photographs taken from the moon's surface the sky above the moon looks dark, yet in many of them no stars are visible. Puzzling as this is, there is an explanation. The photographs that show a dark sky without stars are not photographs of the night sky but of the day sky. On Earth the day sky looks blue because of the way the atmosphere scatters sunlight; the moon has no atmosphere, and so its sky is dark by day as well. But because the moon's surface is very light in color, when sunlight strikes it by day the area above the surface is lit so brightly that the faint light of the stars is hard to make out. Which of the following claims follows from the passage?
  (1) If the moon had an atmosphere, it would not be possible to see stars in its sky even at night
  (2) The atmosphere surrounding the Earth is what enables us to see stars in its night sky
  (3) It is harder to see stars in the moon's night sky than in the Earth's night sky
  (4) If the moon's surface were darker, it would be easier to see stars in its sky by day
EXPL: The brightness of the lit surface is what hides the stars; a darker surface would hide them less. (1), (2) and (3) are not supported.

=== vb-inf_v2_0077 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=3
STEM: Auroras form when high-energy particles penetrate a planet's atmosphere and collide with atoms of gas near its magnetic poles; the collision produces light, usually greenish in color. Unlike the auroras that appear above the Earth, Jupiter's auroras are very strong and never disappear, because Jupiter's powerful magnetic field draws in particles without pause. According to the passage, which of the following statements about the Earth is NOT correct?
  (1) Its auroras are weak in comparison with Jupiter's
  (2) High-energy particles are able to penetrate its atmosphere
  (3) Its magnetic field is stronger than Jupiter's
  (4) Its auroras do not appear without interruption
EXPL: Jupiter's field is described as the powerful one; (1), (2) and (4) each follow from the contrast the passage draws.

=== vb-inf_v2_0078 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=2
STEM: Discussing the vision of the modern city that the architect Le Corbusier presented in 1922, the urban planner Jane Jacobs wrote: "His city is like a wonderful mechanical toy, and its design — as a work of architecture — is simple and harmonious, highly organized and highly clear." Yet with regard to the actual functioning of a city, she claims, his plan "tells nothing but lies": it offers no answer to the problems of managing life in a city, and is no more than an imaginary vision drawn up by someone who does not understand how real cities work. Which of the following best presents Jacobs's opinion of Le Corbusier's plan?
  (1) The impressive features of the city he planned outweigh its shortcomings
  (2) His plan was impressive as architecture, but a city built according to it would not function properly
  (3) At first sight his plan appeared organized and clear, but in practice it was not so at all
  (4) Since he did not understand how real cities work, his design was not sufficiently organized and clear
EXPL: The praise is for the design as architecture and the blame for its working as a city; (3) and (4) turn the praise into an illusion, (1) weighs the two the wrong way.

"Each side in a discussion expects the other to be open to its arguments; but at the same time each side is afraid to admit a mistake, which would be taken as a sign of weakness. Thus discussions often turn into contests whose chief aim is not to be beaten." Which of the following is the question the two excerpts together answer?
  (1) How to prevent the sides in a discussion from entrenching themselves in their positions, and to enable them to reach agreement
  (2) Why it is important to be open to the arguments of the other side in a discussion
  (3) How the way we connect ideas to one another affects our ability to hold productive discussions
  (4) Why disagreements and discussions only rarely end with one side being persuaded by the other
