=== vb-inf_v2_0080 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=4
STEM: At the beginning of the modern era the idea that the human body is like a machine was widespread in the West, and it shaped the development of medicine as well. Just as we tend not to interfere with a machine while it is working properly, so physicians and researchers took no interest in the healthy body, and examined the body chiefly when something in it had ceased to function or had "broken down." Thus medicine in fact developed out of the study of the sick. According to the passage, medicine developed out of the study of the sick —
  (1) because physicians and researchers learned about the healthy body from observing machines, and only disease compelled them to examine an actual body
  (2) because physicians assumed that just as machines tend to break down, so the human body cannot always be healthy
  (3) because comparing the human body to a machine was a mistake that caused many failures in attempts to heal the body
  (4) because, like a machine, the body drew attention only when it stopped working properly
EXPL: The analogy is about when one looks, not about where knowledge comes from; (1) misreads the source of knowledge, (2) invents an assumption, (3) invents a verdict.

It makes life difficult for those who would draw a sharp line between human beings and all other living creatures, but the message of the research is an optimistic one: if human morality has roots in the very distant past, we may expect it to be more resilient in the face of cultural and historical change. Which of the following is NOT implied by the text?
  (1) The fact that bonobos display moral behavior is proof that human morality already existed in humankind's ancient past
  (2) If the origin of morality does not lie in humankind's ancient past, morality may be more vulnerable to cultural and historical influences
  (3) Some of those who would draw a sharp line between human beings and other creatures have held that no other creature displays moral behavior
  (4) Although there appears to be no clear dividing line between human beings and other creatures with regard to morality, human morality may prove more durable than had been thought
EXPL: The text says "if human morality has roots in the distant past" — a condition, not a proof; (2), (3) and (4) each follow from the text's claims.

STEM: The myth of the "normal family" persists despite the sociological and anthropological studies of the family in modern times, and this is due in large part to television series. The harmonious image these series commonly present — of people who meet life's trials without anger and always cooperate with one another — shatters the moment one observes a real family facing its daily problems. It is therefore worrying that therapists often assess the functioning of the families they treat by comparing them with this idealized image. Which of the following best summarizes the passage?
  (1) The task of family therapists is to help the members of a family improve their daily functioning and their ability to cope with problems
  (2) It is true that the characters seen on television create an image that is not wholly accurate, but the therapists' tendency to rely on it is understandable
  (3) Many therapists are influenced by the image of the family created by sociological and anthropological studies
  (4) The standard by which therapists often assess the functioning of the families they treat is unfair, because it is unrealistic

=== vb-inf_v2_0083 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=1
STEM: In 1923 the astronomer Edwin Hubble discovered a Cepheid star in the spiral nebula Andromeda. A few years earlier astronomers had worked out how to calculate the distance between a star of this kind and the Earth, and the discovery therefore enabled Hubble to estimate, for the first time, the distance of Andromeda from the Earth: about nine hundred thousand light-years — far greater than the diameter of the Milky Way, the galaxy in which the Earth lies. In this way it came to be recognized that Andromeda is not part of the Milky Way but a separate galaxy, and hence that the Milky Way is not the only galaxy in the universe. According to the passage, what was the part played by Hubble's discovery?
  (1) It made it possible to apply existing knowledge to calculate the distance of Andromeda, and so to show that Andromeda lies outside the Milky Way
  (2) It showed that Cepheid stars, which are found in Andromeda, are not found in the Milky Way
  (3) It pointed to a new way of calculating the distances of stars from the Earth, and so made it possible to show the size of the Milky Way
  (4) It showed that spiral nebulae are in fact galaxies, and hence it can be inferred that Andromeda, being a spiral nebula, is a galaxy
EXPL: The method already existed; the discovery supplied the star to apply it to. (3) credits Hubble with the method; (2) and (4) reverse the direction of the inference.

=== vb-inf_v2_0084 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=2
STEM: The following appeared in a medical journal: "Unlike the other medications for this disease, the new medication does not attack the concentration of X-type bacteria in the throat — the bacteria that cause the disease — and therefore it does not bring about recovery. It is becoming clear, however, that the medication is necessary for long-term treatment, because it attacks the X bacteria that spread from the throat into the bloodstream while the patient was ill. These bacteria remain in the bloodstream even after recovery, and it has recently been found that they are what cause the disease to recur." Which of the following is implied by the passage?
  (1) The new medication is unique in preventing X bacteria from passing from the throat into the bloodstream
  (2) The medications that attack the X bacteria concentrated in the throat do not prevent the disease from recurring once the patient has recovered
  (3) The new medication can help only patients in whom the disease has already recurred several times
  (4) The fact that the new medication prevents recurrence makes the existing medications unnecessary
EXPL: The existing medications act in the throat, and recurrence comes from the bloodstream. (1) confuses attacking with preventing passage; (3) and (4) go beyond the text.

=== vb-inf_v2_0085 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=2
STEM: The discovery of a twelve-thousand-year-old grave in a cave near the Hilazon stream in the Lower Galilee has aroused great interest among archaeologists. Two archaeologists, Leore Grosman and Natalie Munro, argue that the unusual objects found in the grave show that the hunter-gatherer society that lived in the region had established a social hierarchy of a kind usually associated only with societies of another type — farming societies — which appeared in the region two thousand years later. Which of the following is NOT implied by the passage?
  (1) Objects from ancient graves can help archaeologists to learn about the social structure of ancient societies
  (2) According to Grosman and Munro's findings, farming societies already existed in the Lower Galilee twelve thousand years ago
  (3) Archaeologists did not expect a grave of that age to contain finds of the kind discovered near the Hilazon stream
  (4) The person buried in the grave near the Hilazon stream belonged to a hunter-gatherer society
EXPL: The finding is that a hunter-gatherer society had a hierarchy like the later farmers' — not that farmers were present. (1), (3) and (4) each follow.

=== vb-inf_v2_0086 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=4
STEM: Since the 1950s there have been two main approaches to the study of the language of the writer Haim Hazaz. The first regards the linguistic abundance characteristic of his works as a matter of function — a means of representing the fictional reality faithfully and realistically. According to the second, his language has, besides its basic role as a descriptive instrument, a purpose of its own: it demands that readers immerse themselves in its inherent aesthetics, which are neither bound by nor conditional on the reality it describes. Which of the following questions do the two approaches dispute?
  (1) Are the aesthetics of Hazaz's style bound by the fictional reality of his works, or do they create it?
  (2) Does Hazaz's style contribute to the creation of the fictional world of his works, or only to their aesthetics?
  (3) Is Hazaz's style one of the strong points of his works or one of their weak points?
  (4) Does Hazaz's style have a purpose beyond shaping the world he creates in his works?
EXPL: Both approaches grant the descriptive role; they differ over whether there is anything more. (2) denies the shared ground; (1) and (3) are not the issue.

=== vb-inf_v2_0087 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=3
STEM: In a lecture on the study of the universe the lecturer said: "Everyone knows that when many fish swim in a small aquarium the crowding in it is great, but that if the aquarium is enlarged the crowding lessens. According to one theory, shortly after the universe came into being its volume grew, yet the density of the matter in it remained as it had been; hence, necessarily, the quantity of matter in it grew as well. In the terms of the aquarium example we would say, then, that ____." Which of the following best completes the lecturer's words?
  (1) the number of fish in the aquarium rose, while the size of the aquarium remained as it was
  (2) when the aquarium was enlarged the crowding in it did not change, since the number of fish in it remained as it was
  (3) when the aquarium was enlarged, the number of fish in it grew as well
  (4) the number of fish in the aquarium ceased to affect the crowding in it
EXPL: Volume up and density unchanged means quantity up — more fish in a bigger tank. (1) keeps the tank the same size; (2) keeps the fish the same and so would lower the crowding; (4) breaks the relation the example rests on.

  (3) Why do the young approach an unfamiliar object before the adults?

From Chaim's words it follows that in his view, unlike the study of history, the study of economics ____ finding work easily, ____ enriching a person's inner world.
  (1) does not help / but contributes to
  (2) makes possible / but contributes far less to
  (3) helps / but at the price of renouncing altogether
  (4) does indeed make possible / but contributes much to

  (1) The uniqueness of Israeli architecture lies in its faithful reflection of social, economic and cultural conceptions

  (1) The intellectual level of Dante's political treatise cannot be properly assessed without studying it in depth

Yet at the same time he presents his poetry, which lays bare the dance, as the only language that makes self-expression and genuine communication possible. Which of the following best fits the "inner contradiction" described in the passage?
  (1) Laskly tries to express himself by means of non-verbal instruments, such as the dance
  (2) Laskly called his cycle "Empty Dances," though its claim is that verbal language is empty
  (3) Laskly seeks to describe abstract things, such as thoughts or feelings, by means of something concrete, such as verbal language
  (4) Laskly tries to use words to describe the helplessness of verbal language

  (2) Life in the great city is a life of solitude
  (3) In our day it is hard for a person to be alone

=== vb-inf_v2_0094 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=3
STEM: In a book on globalization it was written: "Globalization is remarkable in that it unites and in that it divides. Alongside the strengthening of the worldwide flow of business, money and information, a separation between populations is being fixed and a barrier raised between those who enjoy the fruits of globalization and those who do not. The mobility that globalization makes possible — social as well as physical — becomes an object of desire, but it is a prized resource that is distributed unequally." Which of the following claims best expresses the central idea of the passage?
  (1) Although globalization seeks to benefit everyone equally, it fails, since not all populations enjoy equal mobility
  (2) Since the advantage of globalization is the worldwide flow of information, and since it harms the mobility of certain populations, there are populations that globalization harms
  (3) Although the worldwide flow of business, money and information has an effect on everyone, it raises a barrier between populations that enjoy great mobility and populations that do not
  (4) The harm done to the mobility of certain populations is what prevents the worldwide flow of business, money and information

=== vb-inf_v2_0095 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=4
STEM: In his book on the openings of stories, Amos Oz wrote: "The title of Chekhov's story 'Rothschild's Fiddle' leads the reader astray in four ways: the Rothschild of the story is not the famous banker, he is not the narrator, the fiddle is not his until the last pages, and he is not the story's hero but only a secondary figure, a poor and wretched clerk." From the passage it emerges that Oz makes three of the following four assumptions. Which is the remaining one?
  (1) When the title of a story mentions a name, the reader assumes it is the name of the story's hero
  (2) The name Rothschild is identified with the famous banker
  (3) From the expression "X's fiddle" one infers that the fiddle belongs to X
  (4) The main figures in stories are usually not poor and wretched people
EXPL: Each of the first three assumptions is needed for one of the "ways" the title misleads; the fourth concerns what happens inside the story, not what the title leads a reader to expect.

=== vb-inf_v2_0096 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=1
STEM: In an article it was written: "All empires decline in the end — an empire lasts about three hundred years on average. Look at Rome: its prosperity drew migrants to it; the new population brought with it new values and customs that undermined the empire's strength, and they brought it down from within. In the United States, which can be regarded as an empire about a hundred and fifty years old, a similar process is to be expected before long." According to the passage, which of the following is NOT expected to happen in the United States?
  (1) It will continue to flourish as an empire for another three hundred years
  (2) A new population will arrive in it
  (3) The values prevailing in it will change
  (4) Internal processes will bring about its collapse
EXPL: At a hundred and fifty years, another three hundred would break the average the passage asserts; (2), (3) and (4) are the stages of the Roman pattern the passage predicts.

=== vb-inf_v2_0097 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=3
STEM: Leah Goldberg gave her opinion on the literary and theoretical problems involved in writing literature for children, and from time to time set out her views on them. She never formulated a "theory of children's literature" in a central, comprehensive essay; but her thinking on these problems was not devoid of systematic order and conceptual consistency. Which of the following sentences best summarizes what is said in the passage?
  (1) Although Leah Goldberg wrote a great deal about the problems of writing for children, she did not write a comprehensive essay defining a "theory of children's literature," because she believed that writing such an essay would raise literary and theoretical problems
  (2) Although Leah Goldberg dealt regularly with the problems of writing for children, and at times set out her opinion on them, she did not put the things down in writing, since her positions were not systematic
  (3) Leah Goldberg held an orderly and consistent conception of children's literature, which she expressed on several occasions, although she never wrote a comprehensive essay presenting her positions on the subject
  (4) Leah Goldberg was aware of the literary and theoretical problems of writing for children, but they did not deter her from engaging in such writing herself
EXPL: "Not devoid of order and consistency" affirms the order; (2) denies it, (1) supplies a motive, (4) changes the subject to her own writing for children.

=== vb-inf_v2_0098 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=2
STEM: The branches of the river Pregel divide the city of Königsberg into four parts, and between the four parts stretch seven bridges. For years the city's inhabitants tried to find a route that would cross each of the seven bridges exactly once, without crossing any bridge twice, and they did not succeed. In 1735 the mathematician Leonhard Euler proved that such a route is impossible. In the article he published following this discovery he named it "the Königsberg bridge problem," and showed that it exists in many situations, theoretical and practical alike. In doing so he laid the foundations of graph theory — a central field of mathematics. Which of the following claims arises from the passage?
  (1) In 1735 graph theory was a new field of mathematics, known to few
  (2) No one ever succeeded in crossing all the bridges of Königsberg without crossing one of them twice
  (3) Euler was the first to show that all the bridges can be crossed if one of them is crossed twice
  (4) In his article Euler showed that other cities divided by rivers have the same bridge problem
EXPL: What is impossible was never done. (1) puts the field before its founding; (3) and (4) are not in the passage.

=== vb-inf_v2_0099 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=1
STEM: Stars, such as the sun, are heavenly bodies that emit radiation; around some of them orbit planets, such as the Earth, which emit no radiation. Scientists estimate that around every star in our galaxy orbit, on average, about 1.6 planets, but they note that this estimate is low. The method of estimation rests on observing stars, most of them far from the Earth: when planets orbit a star they pass between it and the Earth and cause changes in the star's brightness. But such changes can be detected only when the planets are far from the star they orbit and not too small in relation to it. According to the passage, why does the method yield an underestimate?
  (1) Because small planets orbiting a star, or planets close to it, do not change the star's brightness enough for the change to be detected
  (2) Because it is reasonable to suppose that besides the stars we can identify there are stars hard to identify, and planets orbit them too
  (3) Because it is reasonable to suppose that there are also planets small in relation to their star, both far from it and near it, which are only a small fraction of all planets
  (4) Because it rests on observing stars far from the Earth and on changes in their brightness, and is not suited to detecting planets near the Earth
EXPL: Planets the method cannot see are planets the count misses. (2) and (4) concern the stars, not the planets; (3) makes the missed planets few.

=== vb-inf_v2_0100 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=2
STEM: The wings of the cicada — a kind of winged insect — are covered with a sort of microscopic spikes, and when a bacterium tries to settle on them its membrane is stretched between the spikes and torn. Researchers hope to develop in the future an artificial material of similar structure and to coat with it the surfaces on which bacteria settle and from which they pass to human beings — door handles, taps and elevator buttons, for instance. Which of the following claims is correct according to the passage?
  (1) The researchers hope to develop artificial surfaces that can be coated with material taken from cicada wings
  (2) Thanks to the structure of their wings, cicadas are not harmed by the bacteria that settle on them
  (3) Bacteria tend to settle on surfaces covered with microscopic spikes
  (4) The microscopic structure of door handles, taps and elevator buttons resembles that of cicada wings
EXPL: Bacteria that are torn on landing cannot harm the insect. (1) confuses an artificial imitation with the natural material; (3) reverses the effect; (4) describes the surfaces as they are hoped to become.

=== vb-inf_v2_0101 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=3
STEM: According to Aristotle, dialectic and rhetoric are both arts of the use of language. The aim of dialectic is to reach the truth, and it is therefore indifferent to who the speaker and the hearer are — for truth does not depend on the situation in which it is stated. The aim of rhetoric, by contrast, is to persuade, and persuasion is by its nature an activity between persons, bound up with a system of relations between a particular speaker and particular hearers. Which of the following sentences best summarizes the difference between dialectic and rhetoric according to Aristotle?
  (1) Rhetoric is an instrument that serves a particular speaker to persuade a particular hearer, whereas dialectic requires the absence of speaker and hearer in order to reach the truth
  (2) Although both are arts of the use of language, dialectic is indifferent to the situation, whereas rhetoric is indifferent to the truth
  (3) The relation between speaker and hearer is relevant only to rhetoric, whose aim is to persuade, and not to dialectic, whose aim is to reach the truth
  (4) The identity of the speaker and the hearer matters only in rhetoric, since it, unlike dialectic, aims at reaching the truth in a particular situation
EXPL: (1) turns indifference into absence; (2) attributes to rhetoric an indifference the passage does not; (4) gives rhetoric the aim of dialectic.

=== vb-inf_v2_0102 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=3

Which of the following does NOT follow from the passage?

  (2) In the critic's view the acting of television actors should be more restrained than that of stage actors
  (3) The critic did not enjoy the theatrical staging on which the special episode is based
  (4) The plot of the special episode was already familiar to viewers of the series

=== vb-inf_v2_0103 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=2
STEM: The meat industry around the world is responsible for the emission of more greenhouse gases than almost any other source. Which of the following claims is correct according to the passage?
  (1) Even power stations pollute less than the meat industry
  (2) Apart from power stations, no source is responsible for more greenhouse-gas emissions than the meat industry
  (3) In the past the meat industry was the most polluting source, but today it is responsible for only eighteen percent of emissions
  (4) The meat industry is responsible for eighteen percent of emissions, and power stations for the rest

=== vb-inf_v2_0104 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=2
STEM: According to Jean-Paul Sartre, a human being is free without limit. One who makes his freedom depend on external factors — social norms, an ideology, or any other anchor — is fleeing from himself, and thereby fails to realize his freedom in any of his decisions; whoever acts so is not an authentic person. According to Sartre, who is an authentic person?
  (1) A person whose freedom is without limit
  (2) A person who realizes his freedom in his decisions
  (3) A person with a clear ideology and a developed sense of belonging
  (4) A person who acts openly against social norms
EXPL: Freedom without limit is everyone's; authenticity is realizing it. (3) is Sartre's example of flight; (4) confuses defiance of norms with independence of them.

=== vb-inf_v2_0105 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=2
STEM: At the end of the eighteenth century, with the rise of the liberal regime in the United States, the use of pen names in American journalism faded. There is irony in this: the conservative discourse that had prevailed before, with its banner of communal values, was more comfortable for writers who wished to guard their privacy and to hide behind an invented identity than the liberal democracy which, although it raised the individual and the value of privacy on high, could not tolerate an offense against the value of transparency. What is the irony the passage describes?
  (1) It was the conservative discourse, concerned with the public and not with the private, that shaped the norms of privacy protection for writers in the first years of liberal democracy
  (2) The conservative discourse, which set the community at the center, allowed writers a privacy that was no longer possible under the new regime, which supposedly sanctified the individual
  (3) The fading of pen names, which allow a writer to hide behind an invented identity, hints at the liberal regime's preference for the private over the public
  (4) Hiding behind an invented identity, which allowed writers to guard their privacy, was consistent with the conservative communal ideology
EXPL: The irony is the mismatch between a regime's values and their effect. (4) is the half without the mismatch; (1) and (3) invent claims.

=== vb-inf_v2_0106 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=3
STEM: In an experiment, participants watched another person being deceived, and were then asked to tell the deceived person about it. While they watched the deception a rise in their heart rate was recorded, but as soon as they had passed the information to the deceived person their heart rate returned to normal. On the basis of these findings and a number of assumptions, the researchers concluded that an attempt to right a social wrong can have a positive effect on health. Three of the following assumptions are among those the researchers relied on. Which is the remaining one?
  (1) Telling a person that he has been deceived is an attempt to right a social wrong
  (2) The return of the heart rate to normal is a positive effect on health
  (3) When a person watches another being deceived, his heart rate rises
  (4) Telling the deceived person is what caused the participants' heart rate to return to normal
EXPL: That is a finding of the experiment, not an assumption; the other three are the bridges from the findings to the conclusion.

=== vb-inf_v2_0107 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=2
STEM: In his ruling the judge wrote: "I am prepared to assume that the employer did not intend to harm the employee's advancement when he fixed her salary. But the employer's intention is not relevant to the matter before us, since the test of whether there was discrimination is objective and not subjective. To determine whether there was discrimination one must examine the final result as it appears in reality." Which of the following claims arises from the ruling?
  (1) Even if an employee feels discriminated against, one must examine whether the objective data show that the employer intended to discriminate
  (2) It is enough to examine the employee's situation against that of other employees in order to determine whether there was discrimination, even if there was no intention to discriminate
  (3) An employee's advancement may be blocked even when there is no evidence that the employer discriminated against her
  (4) Even if there is no evidence that an employee was intentionally discriminated against, discrimination may be found in her situation if the employer intended to discriminate
EXPL: Intention is irrelevant and result is decisive. (1) and (4) restore intention to the test; (3) is not what the ruling says.

=== vb-inf_v2_0108 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=3
STEM: One of the Islamic orders of the Middle Ages was the Naqshbandiyya. The order was distinguished by "seclusion within society": a person is able to feel God within himself and to concentrate on Him while remaining involved in the affairs of the world. This conception ruled out withdrawal from worldly affairs and created a framework in which political activity is legitimate from the spiritual point of view. Even material possessions are legitimate, and indeed symbolize God's grace, so long as the person is detached from them in spirit and their presence and their absence are alike to him. Which of the following sentences best summarizes the order's distinctive conception?
  (1) A person should concentrate on the worship of God and detach himself from material possessions and political activity
  (2) A person must not withdraw from the affairs of the world, since he could not then hold possessions or engage in politics
  (3) A person should be detached in spirit from the affairs of the world and feel God within himself, but without withdrawing from society
  (4) A person cannot engage in politics or seek possessions unless he feels God within himself
EXPL: (1) is the withdrawal the order rejected; (2) makes possessions the reason for staying; (4) turns a permission into a precondition.

What arises from the passage?
  (1) Astronomers have recently shown that one cannot argue that the universe changes all the time without assuming order and law in it
  (2) Astronomers have recently discovered that the universe changes all the time, and from this discovery they learned which laws operate in it
  (3) From the assumption that the universe changes all the time, astronomers arrived at the conclusion that there is order and law in it
  (4) Astronomers have recently understood that unless they assume that the universe changes all the time, they will not be able to find order and law in it

=== vb-inf_v2_0110 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=2
STEM: Dividing one's attention between two (or more) sources of information is one of the kinds of division that take place in consciousness. Which of the following examples does NOT illustrate any of the kinds of division mentioned in the passage?
  (1) A person translating a speaker's words for an audience while the speaker is speaking
  (2) A person clearing dishes from the table and dropping one of them
  (3) A person driving a car and listening to the radio
  (4) A person who is about to telephone someone, and while looking for the telephone weighs what he will say

=== vb-inf_v2_0111 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=4
STEM: Absolute pitch is the ability to identify at once the exact pitch of a tone, not by relation to another tone. It is usual to distinguish two kinds of absolute pitch — active and passive. Those who possess active absolute pitch, besides being able to identify tones, are also able to produce by singing any tone that is asked of them. What is claimed in the passage about those who possess absolute pitch?
  (1) Those with passive absolute pitch can identify a tone at once but not produce it, whereas those with active absolute pitch can produce a tone but not always identify it
  (2) Those with active absolute pitch can sing any tone without relation to another tone, whereas those with passive absolute pitch can do so only by relation to another
  (3) Those with active absolute pitch can identify and produce tones more accurately than those with passive absolute pitch
  (4) Those of both kinds can identify a tone at once, but only those with active absolute pitch can also sing any tone asked of them
EXPL: "Besides being able to identify" keeps identification for both. (1) takes it from the active, (2) gives the passive a relative ability, (3) turns a difference of kind into one of degree.

=== vb-inf_v2_0112 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=4
STEM: The scholars Fenstermacher and Soltis identify three main approaches to education: the executive approach, in which the teacher's task is to lead the pupil to high achievement; the therapist approach, in which the teacher's task is to help the pupil reach self-realization; and the liberationist approach, in which the teacher's task is to broaden the pupil's horizons and to teach him new ways of thinking. Neil Postman was never among the supporters of the executive approach: his early writings reflect the therapist approach, and his later writings a position that combines it with the liberationist approach. Which of the following best describes Postman's approach in his later writings?
  (1) The teacher should teach the pupil new ways of thinking that will let him broaden his horizons
  (2) The teacher should teach the pupil ways of thinking that will help him realize himself and reach high achievement
  (3) The teacher should help the pupil reach self-realization, even at the price of lower achievement
  (4) The teacher should strive to broaden the pupil's horizons and in that way help him reach self-realization
EXPL: A combination of the therapist and the liberationist; (1) is the liberationist alone, (3) the therapist alone, (2) brings in the executive.

  (1) As a historian Ranke had the skill to recognize decisive moments in his own time, but because of his moderate nature he did not use it to influence the society in which he lived
  (2) Ranke was a moderate man, but his moderation was an obstacle to influencing society, since influence requires daring and resolve, which he lacked
  (3) Because of his moderation Ranke did not succeed in recognizing the important moments in which he could have influenced society
  (4) Thanks to his skill in recognizing decisive moments Ranke had the power to influence society, but as a moderate man he never tried to do so

=== vb-inf_v2_0114 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=1
STEM: A "black body" is a term of physics for an object that absorbs all the radiation that strikes it and reflects none. Nevertheless, a black body emits radiation of its own, whose intensity depends only on its temperature. According to classical physics, a black body would emit at every moment radiation of unlimited intensity — that is, it would emit infinite energy, which is physically impossible. This contradiction led Max Planck to the insights that became the foundation of quantum theory. Which of the following claims does NOT arise from the passage?
  (1) The temperature of a black body does not depend on the radiation it absorbs
  (2) Quantum theory grapples with a contradiction that arises from classical physics
  (3) Physically, a body cannot emit infinite energy
  (4) If a body emits radiation, it emits energy
EXPL: The passage speaks of what the emission depends on, not of what the temperature depends on. (2), (3) and (4) are each stated.

  (1) the weakness of American foreign policy in Carter's time

=== vb-inf_v2_0117 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=3
STEM: The researcher Otto Warburg argued that cells become cancerous when, instead of receiving their energy from the mitochondria — the energy units of the cell — they pass to obtaining it by the process of glycolysis. As a rule this happens when a cell is short of oxygen; but Warburg found that cancer cells obtain their energy by glycolysis even when they have enough oxygen. Warburg's idea was rejected, among other things because of the argument of the researcher Hans Krebs that Warburg's findings are only a symptom of cancer, and that glycolysis takes place in cancer cells because their mitochondria are defective. What was the dispute between Warburg and Krebs?
  (1) Warburg argued that glycolysis takes place in cancer cells because they have much oxygen, whereas Krebs argued that it takes place because their mitochondria are defective
  (2) Warburg argued that the conspicuous symptom of cancer cells is obtaining energy by glycolysis, whereas Krebs argued that the conspicuous symptom is their defective mitochondria
  (3) Warburg argued that obtaining energy by glycolysis is what causes cancer, whereas Krebs argued that the mitochondria of cancer cells are damaged and that this is why they obtain energy by glycolysis
  (4) Warburg argued that glycolysis takes place even when there is enough oxygen, whereas Krebs argued that when there is enough oxygen cells obtain their energy from the mitochondria
EXPL: Cause against symptom. (1) misstates Warburg's reason; (2) makes both speak of symptoms; (4) invents a disagreement about oxygen.

=== vb-inf_v2_0118 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=3
STEM: The British mathematician Irving Good wrote in 1965: "Let us define an ultra-intelligent machine as a machine that can far surpass all the intellectual activities of any person, however clever. Since the design of machines is one of these intellectual activities, an ultra-intelligent machine could design even better machines; there would then be an 'intelligence explosion,' and the intelligence of man would be left far behind. Thus the first ultra-intelligent machine is the last invention that man need ever make." According to what is implied by Good's words, why will man not need to make any invention after the ultra-intelligent machine?
  (1) Because the ultra-intelligent machine will exhaust man's inventive capacity, and he will be unable to invent anything better than it
  (2) Because if the intelligence of man is left behind, no further machines will be invented for man, but only for the machines themselves
  (3) Because the inventive capacity of the machine surpasses that of man, so that it will be able to make any invention better than man could
  (4) Because the machine rests on intelligence, and machines surpass man in cleverness, so that there will be no need for inventions that are not machines
EXPL: The argument runs through the machine's ability to design; (1) makes it a limit on man, (2) and (4) invent consequences.

=== vb-inf_v2_0119 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=1

According to what is implied by the passage, why do anthropologists who study later cultures deal little with material culture?
  (1) Because they are able to learn about the symbols and the way of life of the cultures they study by means of their written history
  (2) Because the information about later cultures that can be obtained from material culture is less than the information about earlier cultures
  (3) Because they are not interested in the way of life and the symbols of the cultures they study, but in other features
  (4) Because the written history of the cultures they study provides little information about their material culture

=== vb-inf_v2_0122 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=4
STEM: In reply to a question she was asked, Professor Brightman said: "Many animals can recognize themselves in a mirror; rats make sounds resembling laughter when they are tickled; parrots and dogs show signs of distress when those close to them die. Which of the following questions is most likely to be the one Professor Brightman was answering?
  (1) What evidence is there that animals have an emotional life?
  (2) Which species of animals have a soul?
  (3) Do animals have a soul and an emotional world?
  (4) Do diseases of the soul exist in animals as well?

=== vb-inf_v2_0123 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=2
STEM: "Invasive species" are animals or plants that were brought to a new place, outside their natural range, and that spread there rapidly and establish themselves. At times local species too spread rapidly, but these are defined as "proliferating species." The distinction between the two is important for the conservation of nature: experts recommend eradicating invasive species at once, but only restraining the proliferation of proliferating ones. According to the passage, which question must be asked in order to decide whether to eradicate a rapidly spreading species?
  (1) Is it spreading quickly, like an invasive species, or less quickly, like a proliferating one?
  (2) Is it a local species, or was it brought from elsewhere?
  (3) Has it already established itself, or has it only begun to spread?
  (4) Can the size of its population be restrained?
EXPL: Origin is the whole distinction; speed, establishment and restrainability are not what separates the two.

=== vb-inf_v2_0124 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=3

Which of the following claims does NOT follow from the passage?

  (3) The Belgian government sat in London because of its cooperation with Britain in the field of diamonds

Which of the following claims is implied by Lemström's words?
  (1) The high electrical conductivity of polar plants is a consequence of the aurora
  (2) Without the electric currents, polar plants would grow faster
  (3) The accelerated growth of polar plants results from electric currents that pass through them thanks to their shape, which makes them better conductors
  (4) The aurora is responsible for the accelerated growth of polar plants

=== vb-inf_v2_0132 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=4
STEM: The psychiatrist Carl Jung noticed more than once, in the course of treatment, that his patients raised ideas and beliefs resembling ideas found in ancient myths, although they had no way of knowing those myths. He concluded from this that there exists a stratum which he called "the collective unconscious," shared by all human beings, from which spring the myths, images and ideas that appear in different cultures around the world and in different periods. How did Jung explain the resemblance between the ideas his patients raised and the ancient myths?
  (1) Although the patients did not know the myths, they had been influenced by later stories based on them
  (2) The collective unconscious absorbs ideas from various cultures, and therefore even those who never knew them recognize them
  (3) The patients had been exposed unconsciously to the ideas in the ancient myths, though one cannot know where
  (4) The stratum shared by all human beings, which produced similar myths in different cultures, also produced these ideas in the minds of the patients

=== vb-inf_v2_0133 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=2
STEM: German researchers have developed a new method for mapping the habitats of the various species of mammals in rain forests. Formerly, researchers hid in the forest for this purpose and made observations; in the new method the flies do the work for them. It turns out that carrion flies feed on the flesh of dead animals and retain the DNA of those animals in their bodies. The researchers catch the flies, analyze the DNA in their bodies and so discover what they have eaten; and since carrion flies do not fly far from the carcass, the researchers can know which species live in each area. In the new method, what two things do the researchers do in order to know which species live in each area?
  (1) Analysis of the DNA in the bodies of the flies and of the mammals
  (2) Recording the place where the flies were caught, and analysis of the DNA in their bodies
  (3) Recording the place where the mammals were caught, and analysis of the DNA in their bodies
  (4) Observation in the forest, and mapping of the flies' habitats
EXPL: The place of capture gives the "where," the DNA gives the "who"; the mammals themselves are never handled.

=== vb-inf_v2_0134 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=1
STEM: Questions 134–135 refer to the following news item: "Although the treasury's demand for a reform of the tax policy aroused anger among social organizations, which saw in it harm to the weaker classes, the minister of welfare chose to support it — not because he shrank from a struggle with those classes, or because he shut his eyes to the expected consequences of the reform, but because he knew that if he opposed it he would be unable to spare his ministry's budget a real cut; and so long as his ministry's budget is not cut, he thought, the damage done by the reform will be small." According to the item, which of the following steps is likely to cause the minister of welfare to withdraw his support for the reform?
  (1) An announcement by the minister of the treasury that the cut in the welfare budget will be carried out even if the reform is approved
  (2) A meeting with the leaders of the weaker classes, at which he will set out the damage the reform will do them

  (4) A conversation with the prime minister, in which the prime minister will stress the importance of helping the weaker classes

=== vb-inf_v2_0135 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=4
STEM: Which of the following summarizes the news item above most precisely?
  (1) The social organizations are angry at the minister of welfare's support for the tax reform, though it is known that he is committed to the weaker classes; his support stems from his belief that otherwise a large cut will be made in his budget
  (2) The minister of welfare knew that if he opposed the reform his budget would be cut, and supported it only because he feared the damage of the cut; in response, the social organizations argued that he was ignoring the harm the reform would do to the weak
  (3) Unlike the social organizations, which opposed the reform, the minister of welfare understood that it would harm the weak; he agreed to support it only because he thought that supporting it was the only way to prevent a large cut in his budget
  (4) The minister of welfare knew that if he opposed the reform his budget would be cut; since he believed that the cut would harm the weak more than the reform would, he supported the reform despite the opposition of the social organizations
EXPL: (2) reverses the order of the anger and the support; (3) says the organizations did not understand the harm, which the item contradicts; (1) leaves out the comparison of harms that is the minister's reasoning.

=== vb-inf_v2_0136 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=4
STEM: Both the coffee plant and the tea plant contain caffeine, the stimulating substance. As is well known, drinking a cup of coffee has a much stronger stimulating effect than drinking a cup of tea, and so it is easy to suppose that coffee beans contain more caffeine than tea leaves. In fact, a hundred grams of coffee beans contain less caffeine than a hundred grams of tea leaves. Drinking a cup of coffee is more stimulating than drinking a cup of tea not because the concentration of caffeine in coffee beans is higher, but because the quantity of coffee needed to prepare a cup is much larger than the quantity of tea needed for a cup. Which of the following claims arises from the passage?
  (1) The stimulating effect of coffee does not stem from the caffeine it contains
  (2) Although we think that drinking coffee is more stimulating than drinking tea, in fact drinking tea is more stimulating
  (3) The concentration of caffeine in the plant from which a drink is made has no effect on the drink's stimulating power
  (4) Had the quantities of tea and of coffee needed for a cup been equal, drinking tea would have been more stimulating than drinking coffee
EXPL: Equal quantities would leave the higher concentration of the tea leaves to decide. (1) and (3) deny caffeine any role; (2) contradicts "as is well known."

=== vb-inf_v2_0137 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=1

"Cell expulsion" is the process of expelling surplus substance from the cell: the surplus is packed into small vesicles that are sent to the cell's membrane; the membrane of the vesicle fuses with the membrane of the cell, and the contents of the vesicle are released outside. Which of the following claims is made in the passage?
  (1) The membranes of the vesicles used in expulsion become part of the membrane of the cell
  (2) When a cell takes in a foreign body, liquid is taken in with it
  (3) By drinking, the cell takes back the substances that left it by expulsion
  (4) Some cells ingest and others expel
EXPL: "Fuses with the membrane of the cell" says so; (2), (3) and (4) are not stated.

Which of the following is the central claim of the passage regarding historians today?
  (1) They engage less than in the past in the study of unique, one-time events

=== vb-inf_v2_0141 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=4
STEM: Until recently chemists relied on the "Overton rule," according to which the more lipophilic a substance is — that is, the better it dissolves in fats — the more easily it penetrates the membranes of the body's cells. New technological developments made it possible to test the rule: researchers compared four substances and found that there is no direct relation between their lipophilicity and their rate of penetration into cells, and that in fact the reverse of what the rule predicts holds. In their view, if this finding is valid for many other substances as well, it will be possible to develop drugs that penetrate cells much faster than today's. According to the passage, how will drugs that penetrate cells faster be developed?
  (1) The new drugs will be based on the substances whose rate of penetration was found to be fastest
  (2) The new drugs will be composed of substances that dissolve well in fats and so penetrate cells more easily than the substances used until now
  (3) The new drugs will be based on the technologies the researchers used to test the rule
  (4) The new drugs will be composed of substances of low lipophilicity, in place of substances of high lipophilicity
EXPL: If the reverse of the rule holds, low lipophilicity is what penetrates. (2) is the old rule; (1) and (3) confuse the four test substances and the instruments with the principle.

=== vb-inf_v2_0142 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=3
STEM: In the study of behavior, the accepted distinction was formerly between innate patterns of behavior, whose source is the genetic endowment the individual receives from its parents, and acquired patterns, whose source is the environment in which the individual grew. Today researchers avoid this simple classification, since many studies have shown that most patterns of behavior are the product of the two factors acting together. The classic question that researchers used to ask about a pattern of behavior — "Is this behavior innate or acquired?" — has therefore been replaced by another question. Which of the following is best suited to be that "other question"?
  (1) Can this behavior be innate and acquired at one and the same time?
  (2) Which of the two factors is responsible for this behavior?
  (3) What is the contribution of heredity to this behavior, and what is the contribution of the environment?
  (4) How did the individual acquire the acquired aspects of this behavior?
EXPL: If both factors act together, the question is how much each contributes. (1) and (2) keep the either/or; (4) keeps the old category.

=== vb-inf_v2_0143 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=2
STEM: Although language is an astonishing ability, unique to man among all living things, it does not oblige us to take the study of man out of the domain of biology, since a wonderful ability unique to one animal is not at all a unique thing in the animal kingdom. Among the wonders of nature we are no more than one kind in the series of primates, with a talent of our own to display. Which of the following claims is the central claim of the author of the passage?
  (1) Comparing human language with the wonderful abilities of other animals shows that man is not like every animal
  (2) The fact that of all living things only human beings have language does not mean that human beings differ in essence from the other animals
  (3) Certain animals are endowed with abilities superior to man's, and man should therefore not be regarded as exceptional
  (4) Certain animals — human beings, bats and migratory birds, for example — have wonderful abilities that no other animal has
EXPL: (4) is the evidence, not the claim; (3) turns "unique" into "superior"; (1) reverses the conclusion.

=== vb-inf_v2_0144 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=1
STEM: What is literature? This is a hard question, and theorists have struggled with it a great deal without success. The reason is clear: in many cases it is easier to see what a text that is not regarded as literature has in common with literature than what it has in common with other works defined as literature. Some novels, for example, resemble collections of historical documents more than they resemble lyrical poetry, and Shakespeare's plays resemble poetry more than they resemble novels. According to the passage, why is it hard to answer the question "What is literature?"
  (1) Because it is hard to point to features common to all works of literature that distinguish them from texts that are not literature
  (2) Because there are indeed outstanding works of literature, but also texts that are hard to classify
  (3) Because it is possible to define genres of literature — the novel, the play, the poem — but not the broad field called "literature"
  (4) Because it can be argued that a collection of historical documents, or the titles of documents, are literature
EXPL: The examples show works within literature that resemble outsiders more than insiders — no common boundary. (3) is a consequence; (2) and (4) are not the reason given.

=== vb-inf_v2_0145 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=1
STEM: Newton's theory of forces describes simply and precisely the motion of bodies, provided that they do not approach the speed of light; and his theory of gravitation is usable as long as it refers to a system in which the force of gravity is weak. Which of the following claims is made in the passage?
  (1) Unlike Newton's description, the special theory of relativity faithfully describes the motion of bodies approaching the speed of light, and the general theory faithfully describes a system in which the force of gravity is not weak
  (2) It is rare to find a physical system that both Newton's description and Einstein's theories of relativity describe faithfully
  (3) Newton's theories are indeed more precise than Einstein's, but under certain conditions they cannot be used
  (4) Newton's theory of forces is more precise than his theory of gravitation, though less usable, but both yield a false picture in the absence of certain conditions
EXPL: Each Einsteinian theory takes over exactly where the corresponding Newtonian one fails. (2) contradicts the passage — where Newton holds, relativity holds too; (3) and (4) invent comparisons of precision.

  (4) dragons / the shapes of lizards

=== vb-inf_v2_0147 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=2
STEM: When a patient who receives a treatment believes that it will help him, a real improvement in his condition sometimes occurs, even if the treatment is a mere dummy — sugar pills given as a real medicine. This phenomenon, called the "placebo effect," is affected by the patient's expectations, and its strength varies accordingly: receiving an injection of a dummy substance, for example, leads to greater improvement than taking dummy pills, and it seems the reason is that an injection is perceived as a more drastic medical intervention. Likewise it was found that sugar pills presented to patients as an expensive medicine are "more effective" than sugar pills presented as a cheap one. According to the passage, how can the finding described in the last sentence be explained?
  (1) Taking an expensive medicine is perceived as a less drastic intervention, and patients expect its effect to be smaller
  (2) Patients assume there is a match between the cost of a treatment and its effectiveness, and the difference in expectations produces the difference in effect
  (3) The placebo effect causes the taking of sugar pills, though they are a mere dummy, to bring real improvement
  (4) The chemical composition of the sugar pills presented as expensive differs from that of the pills presented as cheap
EXPL: The passage's mechanism is expectation; price shapes expectation. (1) reverses the direction; (3) restates the phenomenon; (4) contradicts "the same sugar pills."

  (3) The quantity of fuel it produces is larger than the quantity it consumes

=== vb-inf_v2_0153 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=1
STEM: The eighteenth-century Scottish scholar James Hutton was the first to argue that the geological processes taking place today — volcanic activity, for instance, or the sinking of sediments to the sea floor — have taken place in the same way since time immemorial. From this Hutton derived the idea that the huge geological formations, such as mountains and strata of rock, were created in a slow, continuous process over millions of years. The idea contradicted the view common in his day, that the face of the earth had not changed since the creation. Which of the following sentences best summarizes Hutton's idea?
  (1) The slow geological processes taking place today have been taking place for millions of years, and they are what shaped the face of the earth
  (2) The fact that geological processes are slow and continuous contradicts the view that the face of the earth has not changed
  (3) Millions of years ago the face of the earth took shape in a slow, continuous process, as it does today
  (4) Mountains, valleys and strata of rock are created by volcanic activity or the sinking of sediments — geological processes that have acted in the same way since time immemorial
EXPL: The idea has two halves — the sameness of the processes and their cumulative effect — and only (1) holds both. (2) states the consequence; (3) and (4) each drop a half.

  (2) Moreover, many companies are engaged in developing vehicles that do not run on petrol, and only when the dependence on it ends will the damage to the environment be reduced

=== vb-inf_v2_0155 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=1

On which of the following questions do the scholars and Jaynes disagree?

  (2) What did the ancient Egyptians mean by the "voice" and the "tongue" of the god Ptah?
  (3) Should one infer from the inscription that the ancient Egyptians' hallucinations came from Ptah?
  (4) Did the ancient Egyptians think that Ptah created nature, or that he created voices and hallucinations?
EXPL: Both sides agree on (2) as the question; they differ on its answer. (3) and (4) misstate the positions.

=== vb-inf_v2_0157 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=2
STEM: In an article published in 2000 it was written: "In recent decades the average temperature in our region has changed: from relatively high values in the 1950s it fell to lower values in the 1970s, did not change noticeably in the 1980s, rose steeply in the course of the 1990s, and finally reached a level higher than its level in the 1950s. On the whole there is a correspondence between the change in the average temperature in our region and the change in the world's average temperature in this period. Nevertheless, in our region the difference between the temperatures at the end of the 1990s and those of the 1950s is not as large as it is in the world graph." Which of the following claims is certainly correct according to the data above?
  (1) At the end of the 1990s the average temperature in our region was lower than the world's average temperature
  (2) At the end of the 1990s the world's average temperature was higher than it had been in the 1950s
  (3) In our region the average temperature throughout the 1990s was higher than the average temperature in the 1950s
  (4) In our region the gap between the average temperature in the 1950s and the average temperature in the 1970s was larger than the gap between the 1950s and the end of the 1990s
EXPL: The region ended above its 1950s level, and its rise is called smaller than the world's — so the world's rise from the 1950s is larger still. === vb-inf_v2_0168 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=2
STEM: Willard Quine, one of the prominent philosophers of science, argued against Thomas Kuhn, who was regarded as his successor, that Kuhn's method leads to a dead end. According to the passage, which of the following claims fits Quine's approach?
  (1) The very occupation with the question "does knowledge have justification?" leads to a dead end
  (2) Knowledge has no external justification, but it has an internal justification, and therefore it is possible to determine which of our knowledge is correct
  (3) One must avoid the conclusion that it is impossible to determine which of our knowledge is correct, even at the price of reaching a dead end
  (4) From the fact that knowledge has no internal justification it follows that it needs no external justification either

=== vb-inf_v2_0172 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=1
STEM: Thucydides, the Greek historian of the fifth century BCE, explained in what way his book "The History of the Peloponnesian War" differs from the writings of the chroniclers who preceded him: "The chroniclers wrote things pleasant to the ear at the expense of the truth; their stories cannot be checked, and since years have passed, most of them have entered the domain of myth. It may be that my story, which has nothing of myth in it, will not please the ear of its hearers. But I shall be content if those who care for the truth find benefit in it — for this book was written as a possession for all generations, and not as an entertainment for an hour." Which of the following sentences best summarizes Thucydides' explanation?
  (1) The chroniclers wished to please the readers of their own time, and therefore wrote so that their stories would suit their ears; Thucydides wrote his book so that it would serve future generations as well
  (2) In order that their stories would interest readers, the chroniclers turned to the writing of myths and refrained from historical events that could be investigated
  (3) The stories of the chroniclers may be pleasant to read, but they deal with events of little importance, and their benefit is therefore less than that of Thucydides' book
  (4) The descriptions of events in the chroniclers' writings may be more reliable than those in Thucydides' book, but his descriptions are more instructive
EXPL: (2) makes the myth-making deliberate; (3) and (4) attribute to the chroniclers importance and reliability the passage denies them.

=== vb-inf_v2_0173 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=3
STEM: According to psychoanalytic theory, when we read a literary work we construct its "subtext" — what we, as "writers" of the work's meaning, add to what is written explicitly. According to this approach, the insights that arise from a work are bound up with the deep tension between what the work does not say and the things that are supposedly no less important than what it does say. The intention is not to deny what the work says, but to examine, as it were, the comparison: in what sense the work is not entirely what it appears to be. Which of the following sentences best summarizes the psychoanalytic approach to the reading of a literary work?
  (1) Reading a literary work does not mean seeking what the work explicitly says or does not say, but following the thought processes of the writer and the subtext he constructed
  (2) Reading a literary work means examining the distinction between what the work tries to say and the subtext its readers construct, which turns the work into something other than what it appears
  (3) The construction of the subtext takes place in the course of reading, and its meaning is not a denial of the text but the exposure of a layer hidden in what is not said explicitly
  (4) The construction of the subtext in the course of reading is an attempt to reveal the things the writer tried to hide, and so to understand what he really intended
EXPL: The readers construct, the text is not denied, the layer is in what is unsaid. (1) and (4) give the subtext to the writer; (2) turns the tension into a transformation.

=== vb-inf_v2_0176 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=1

=== vb-inf_v2_0179 [real exam | set inf_v2 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=1

The formula finally adopted was that they would be "partners in a vision" of reducing greenhouse gases within forty years, and they regarded it as great progress compared with the formula adopted four years earlier: "to relate seriously" to the task of reducing emissions. Which of the following claims is NOT implied by the passage?

  (3) The software can reconstruct a sound from a video only if the object that emitted the sound appears in the video

  (4) because the pupils tried to reproduce their teacher's playing exactly

=== vb-inf_v1_0052 [inf_v1 REVIEW | set inf_v1 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=3
STEM: From a review of a book on the history of medicine: "Dr. Halevi writes with equal command of clinical practice and of the history of ideas, and so she is able to show how a change in what physicians believed about the body altered what they did at the bedside. She can do this because she trained in both fields — something that the strict separation between medical schools and history departments makes almost impossible today." Which of the following claims is implied by the reviewer?
  (1) The separation between medical schools and history departments has produced more books like Dr. Halevi's, not fewer
  (2) Dr. Halevi's account would have been as convincing had she trained in only one of the two fields
  (3) Were it not for the separation between medical schools and history departments, more books like Dr. Halevi's would be written
  (4) Had Dr. Halevi studied the two fields in the same department, she could not have connected belief and practice
EXPL: (1) reverses the reviewer's complaint; (2) contradicts 'because she trained in both'; (4) inverts the point about the separation.

=== vb-inf_v1_0053 [inf_v1 REVIEW | set inf_v1 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=4
STEM: Professor Adar: "If the excavation at the eastern gate had not turned up the inscription last spring, I would still be among those who doubt Professor Katz's dating of the city wall." Which of the following is NOT implied by Professor Adar's remark?
  (1) Professor Adar no longer doubts Professor Katz's dating of the wall
  (2) The inscription found at the eastern gate bears on the dating of the wall
  (3) Before last spring, Professor Adar doubted Professor Katz's dating
  (4) Professor Katz took part in the excavation at the eastern gate last spring
EXPL: The remark presupposes a change of mind, the inscription's relevance and earlier doubt; it says nothing about who excavated.

=== vb-inf_v1_0054 [inf_v1 REVIEW | set inf_v1 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=3
STEM: Maya told Oren that she had read about a study recommending that runners drink coffee before a race, because caffeine raises endurance. She added that she does not intend to follow the recommendation, since coffee upsets her stomach and other studies she has read contradict this one. From Oren's reply, Maya realized that he had not understood her. Which of the following is most likely to have been Oren's reply?
  (1) "Then I am glad I did not read the article myself — I am easily swayed by studies and would surely have started drinking coffee before every run."
  (2) "I would do as you do; it would take more than one study to make me drink something that disagrees with me."
  (3) "I am actually thinking of trying it — if all the studies point the same way, a little stomach trouble is a small price to pay."
  (4) "Knowing you, even without the other studies you would not have touched coffee before a race."
EXPL: Only (3) rests on a false premise — Maya said the studies contradict one another; the other replies track what she said.

=== vb-inf_v1_0055 [inf_v1 REVIEW | set inf_v1 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=2
STEM: A librarian was asked how many of the books in the reading room are borrowed at least once a year. She answered: "Fewer than you think — and if you counted the books that are borrowed at least once a month, the number would be smaller still." A visitor then asked her a question, to which she replied: "That one I cannot answer without a full count, since a book may be borrowed once and never again, or twenty times in a year." Which of the following is most likely to be the visitor's question?
  (1) How many books are there in the reading room altogether?
  (2) How many times in a year is a book in the reading room borrowed, on average?
  (3) Are books borrowed more often in winter than in summer?
  (4) How many books were borrowed at least once last year?
EXPL: An average needs the number of borrowings per book, which the librarian says varies from one to twenty; her earlier answers show she can count (1) and (4), and (3) is not about frequency per book.

=== vb-inf_v1_0056 [inf_v1 REVIEW | set inf_v1 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=3
STEM: Two findings emerged from a study of how the color of a room affects people working in it: those who worked in a room painted blue made fewer errors in a proofreading task than those in a room painted yellow; and those in the yellow room produced more ideas in a brainstorming task than those in the blue room. On the basis of these findings the researchers drew up several recommendations. Which of the following is least likely to be one of them?
  (1) The room in which the accounts are checked should be painted blue rather than yellow
  (2) The color of a room should be chosen according to the kind of work that is done in it
  (3) A person who is prone to careless errors should be moved to a yellow room
  (4) Meetings intended to generate proposals should be held in a yellow room rather than a blue one
EXPL: The findings link yellow with more ideas but more errors; sending an error-prone person into a yellow room contradicts them, while (1), (2) and (4) follow.

=== vb-inf_v1_0057 [inf_v1 REVIEW | set inf_v1 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=2
STEM: In the old harbor towns, a captain's share of a voyage's profit was fixed not by the size of his ship but by the distance of the voyage from home: the farther the port of destination, the larger the share. We can understand from this why the captains who sailed the short coastal routes, whose ships were often the largest in the fleet, received smaller shares than the captains of the small vessels that traded with the distant islands. Based on the above, why did the captains of the coastal routes receive smaller shares?
  (1) because their ships were larger than the vessels that traded with the islands
  (2) because their voyages were shorter than the voyages to the islands
  (3) because the coastal trade brought smaller profits than the island trade
  (4) because they carried cargo of lower value than the island traders carried
EXPL: The rule ties the share to distance alone; (1) mentions a fact the rule ignores, and (3) and (4) supply reasons the passage never gives.

  (1) A planet passing in front of its star blocks a small part of the star's light for a few hours
  (2) A planet in orbit passes in front of its star at regular intervals
  (3) Regular dimming of a star can also be caused by dark patches on the star's own surface
  (4) Astronomers have almost no other means of detecting a planet around a distant star than measuring the star's light
EXPL: (1), (2) and (4) together explain why regular dimming is worth pursuing; (3) gives a rival cause and, if anything, argues against the interest.

=== vb-inf_v1_0200 [inf_v1 REVIEW | set inf_v1 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=4
STEM: Researchers find that people who work at a screen for long hours suffer eye strain, and locate the cause in two habits: focusing at a fixed short distance, and blinking less than usual. They therefore recommend the "20-20-20 rule": every twenty minutes, look at something twenty meters away for twenty seconds. Which of the following does NOT follow from the researchers' account?
  (1) Looking at a distant object relaxes the focusing muscles that a screen keeps fixed
  (2) A person who blinks normally while reading a screen would suffer less strain
  (3) A person who works at a screen for short periods with long breaks would suffer less strain than one who works continuously
  (4) Eye strain is caused by the brightness of the screen, so dimming the screen would remove it
EXPL: The account names distance and blinking, not brightness; (1)–(3) each follow from one of the two causes.

STEM: It is often said that the great cathedrals were built by faith. The size of a cathedral is a better guide to a town's wealth than to its piety. Which of the following is the main claim of the passage?
  (1) The great cathedrals were built by faith, though money was also needed
  (2) The size of a cathedral reflects the wealth of its town more than it reflects its piety
  (3) Devout towns that were poor built small churches
  (4) Tolls on pilgrims were the main source of the money that built the cathedrals
EXPL: (1) keeps the claim the passage sets out to correct; (3) is a supporting example; (4) promotes one item of a list to the main source.

=== vb-inf_v1_0310 [inf_v1 REVIEW | set inf_v1 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=1
STEM: In the bakery, loaves are sold in the order they were baked, the earliest first; the exception is any loaf ordered in advance, which is set aside for its customer whatever its baking time. The last loaf baked this morning was sold first. Which of the following can be inferred from the text?
  (1) The last loaf baked this morning had been ordered in advance
  (2) The customer who bought the last loaf baked was the first customer to arrive
  (3) No loaf baked this morning before the last one had been ordered in advance
  (4) The last loaf baked this morning was sold before it had cooled
EXPL: (2) confuses order of arrival with order of sale; (3) does not follow — other loaves may also have been ordered; (4) is about time, which the text does not give.

=== vb-inf_v1_0311 [inf_v1 REVIEW | set inf_v1 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=1
STEM: Two farms on the same hillside, planted with the same apple variety in the same year, yield very differently: the upper farm, which is colder, harvests three weeks later but its apples keep through the winter; the lower farm harvests early but its apples must be sold within a month. Which of the following is implied in the text?
  (1) The colder conditions of the upper farm are connected with its apples' keeping longer
  (2) The upper farm's apples are sweeter than the lower farm's because they ripen more slowly
  (3) The lower farm harvests more apples than the upper farm because it harvests earlier
  (4) The lower farm's apples do not keep because they are picked before they are ripe
EXPL: (2), (3) and (4) each add a fact — sweetness, quantity, ripeness — that the passage does not give; only the link between cold and keeping is implied by the pairing.

  (2) How many volunteers founded the brigade?
  (3) How many fires did the brigade attend in the year it became a paid service?
  (4) How many engines does the brigade have today?

=== vb-inf_v1_0313 [inf_v1 REVIEW | set inf_v1 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=3
STEM: Of the town's two markets, the Tuesday market is the older and the larger, but the Saturday market, though smaller, is the one that draws visitors from outside the town, since it sells crafts rather than food. According to the passage, which of the following statements is not correct?
  (1) The Tuesday market is larger than the Saturday market
  (2) The Saturday market sells crafts, and the Tuesday market sells food
  (3) The Saturday market is older than the Tuesday market
  (4) Visitors from outside the town come to the Saturday market rather than the Tuesday market
EXPL: The Tuesday market is the older; (2) is a fair reading of "crafts rather than food," and (1) and (4) are stated.

=== vb-inf_v1_0314 [inf_v1 REVIEW | set inf_v1 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=3
STEM: Yael told Noam that the clinic had advised her to take a two-week course of tablets for her cough, but that she would take only the first week, since the tablets make her drowsy and her cough was already gone. From Noam's reply, Yael saw that he had misunderstood her. Which of the following is most likely to have been Noam's reply?
  (1) "Two weeks of drowsiness is a lot to ask when the cough has already gone."
  (2) "A week seems enough if the cough has gone — the clinic tends to prescribe for the worst case."
  (3) "So you are stopping the tablets altogether? I would at least finish the first week."
  (4) "Drowsy or not, I would take the second week too — coughs come back."
EXPL: Only (3) rests on a false premise — she said she would take the first week; (1), (2) and (4) all track what she said, whether or not they agree with her.

=== vb-inf_v1_0350 [inf_v1 REVIEW | set inf_v1 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=1

  (1) The newspaper became a daily in the year a steam press was installed
  (2) The newspaper became a daily when it absorbed its only rival
  (3) The bookseller who founded the newspaper wrote all of it himself
  (4) The newspaper has been printed in the city since it became a daily
EXPL: (2) attaches the change to the wrong event; (3) turns "most" into "all"; (4) moves the printing to the wrong year.

=== vb-inf_v1_0351 [inf_v1 REVIEW | set inf_v1 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=3
STEM: The lock on the canal was rebuilt in 1904 to take the longer barges then coming into use; its gates, of oak, lasted until 1962, when they were replaced in steel, and the steel gates have been repainted every five years since. A keeper lived in the cottage beside the lock until 1970; it is now let to holidaymakers. Which of the following is NOT stated in the text?
  (1) The oak gates lasted for more than fifty years
  (2) The steel gates have been repainted at regular intervals since they were installed
  (3) The steel gates have needed no repair since they were installed
  (4) The lock was rebuilt because longer barges had come into use
EXPL: Repainting is stated; freedom from repair is not. (1) follows from the dates, (2) and (4) are stated.

=== vb-inf_v1_0352 [inf_v1 REVIEW | set inf_v1 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=1

Islanders who fall ill on other days must travel to the port, and in winter the road to the northern village is often closed. Which of the following is implied in the text?
  (1) In winter there are Mondays on which the doctor cannot reach the northern village
  (2) In winter the doctor visits the northern village on a different day of the week
  (3) The clinic in the port has no doctor of its own on Mondays, Wednesdays and Fridays
  (4) Islanders from the northern village travel to the port more often than islanders from the other villages
EXPL: (2) invents an arrangement; (3) goes beyond "the doctor spends the other days at the clinic"; (4) is not supported by anything said about frequency.

  (3) The village bakes its own bread on the other days of the week
  (4) The village's bread was better than the bakery's

=== vb-inf_v1_0354 [inf_v1 REVIEW | set inf_v1 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=2
