It is easy to see why the pilots of the northern channel, whose ships were small fishing vessels, earned more than the pilots of the straight southern channel, who guided the great grain ships. Based on the above, why did the northern pilots earn more?
  (1) because the fishing vessels they guided were more numerous than the grain ships
  (2) because the northern channel had more turns and shoals than the southern
  (3) because the grain ships paid a fixed fee whatever the channel
  (4) because the northern pilots guided more ships in a day, the vessels being small
EXPL: (1) and (4) substitute number for difficulty; (3) invents a fee arrangement.

=== vb-inf_v1_0355 [inf_v1 REVIEW | set inf_v1 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=3

  (2) Chewing stimulates the flow of saliva, which washes food from the teeth
  (3) The gum is sold in three flavors, of which mint is the most popular with children
  (4) Most patients will chew gum after a meal far more readily than they will brush

=== vb-inf_v1_0356 [inf_v1 REVIEW | set inf_v1 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=1

  (2) Dafna believes the shop stays open late because people who work evening shifts read more than others
  (3) Dafna believes the shop will close again once the evening shifts end
  (4) Dafna believes the bookshop's owner works an evening shift himself

=== vb-inf_v1_0357 [inf_v1 REVIEW | set inf_v1 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=2
STEM: The old proverb says that a watched pot never boils. It boils, of course, at exactly the same moment whether watched or not; what the watching changes is the watcher, for whom minutes of attention pass more slowly than minutes of distraction. The proverb is not about kettles but about the elasticity of felt time. Which of the following is the main claim of the passage?
  (1) A watched pot really does take longer to boil than an unwatched one
  (2) Time seems to pass more slowly to a person who is attending to something than to one who is distracted
  (3) Proverbs that seem to be about objects are usually about people
  (4) A pot boils at the same moment whether it is watched or not
EXPL: (1) is what the passage denies; (3) generalizes from one proverb to all; (4) is a supporting fact, not the claim.

=== vb-inf_v1_0358 [inf_v1 REVIEW | set inf_v1 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=1

  (1) Gadi's referral letter was marked "urgent"
  (2) No other patient in the queue had a referral letter marked "urgent"
  (3) Gadi was the only patient who had not been called by the time he arrived
  (4) The patients ahead of Gadi had arrived without referral letters
EXPL: (2) does not follow — others may have been urgent and already called; (3) contradicts "arrived last"; (4) confuses having a letter with having an urgent one.

=== vb-inf_v1_0360 [inf_v1 REVIEW | set inf_v1 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=3
STEM: Of the two clocks in the square, the church clock is the older but the town-hall clock is the more accurate, since it was regulated by the railway when the line reached the town, and it is by the town-hall clock that the market opens.   (1) The town-hall clock is more accurate than the church clock
  (2) The church clock is older than the town-hall clock
  (3) The market opens by the church clock, which is the older of the two
  (4) The town-hall clock was regulated by the railway

=== vb-inf_v1_0361 [inf_v1 REVIEW | set inf_v1 | Paragraph Understanding] answer=3
STEM: Researchers find that gardeners who water in the evening lose less water to evaporation than those who water at noon, and that plants watered at the root take up more than plants sprayed from above. They recommend evening watering at the root. Which of the following does NOT follow from their account?
  (1) A gardener who waters at noon loses more of the water he applies to evaporation than one who waters in the evening
  (2) Plants that are sprayed from above receive less of the water applied than plants watered at the root
  (3) Plants that are watered in the evening need less water than plants watered at noon
  (4) Evening watering at the root delivers more of the water applied than noon spraying from above
EXPL: The account concerns how much of the applied water is lost or taken up, not how much the plant needs; (1), (2) and (4) each restate a finding.
